# sNeek Property Services — System Reference

> **The master document for this platform.** It describes the whole system — every portal, feature, and mechanism — and is kept current as the system changes.
>
> **Maintenance protocol (standing rule, set 2026-07-27):** any new feature, fix, or modification MUST update this document in the relevant section(s) *in the same commit as the code*. Tag newly added behaviour with a month marker, e.g. "(2026-08)". Existing "(2026-07)" tags mark the July 2026 overhaul waves; untagged text is base behaviour that predates them. Append a one-line entry to the Change Log at the bottom for each shipped wave.

**What this system is:** the operations platform for sNeek Property Services (Sydney, Australia) — Airbnb turnover cleaning, end-of-lease/bond cleans, regular home cleaning, and a linen/laundry service. Five role-based portals (Admin/Ops, Cleaner, Client, Laundry driver, QA inspector) over one Next.js 14 + Prisma + PostgreSQL application. The v2 "Estate" UI is the default experience; v1 remains as legacy fallback.

**Document map**

| Section | Covers |
|---|---|
| **A. Platform Foundation** | Architecture, auth & roles, impersonation, settings, notifications, uploads, scheduler, GPS/Live Ops, audit, integrations, SaaS state |
| **B. Jobs, Forms & the Cleaner Portal** | Job lifecycle, properties, iCal sync & timing rules, forms/checklists/builder, the 5-stage cleaner journey, drafts, submissions & reports, admin job surfaces |
| **C. Laundry & QA/Accountability** | Laundry scheduling engine, key-lost mode, task lifecycle, driver portal & routes, reminders; QA assignments, inspections, scoring, rework, QA pay, accountability, Approval Center |
| **D. Money, Client Portal & Marketing** | Pricing & quotes, client invoicing, the four pay streams & two settlement rails, cleaner/QA invoicing, payroll runs, the client experience, marketing hub |

---
## Section A — Platform Foundation & Cross-Cutting Systems

### A1. Architecture overview

**Ollama setup and capability diagnostics (2026-09, September 28).** Admin Settings → System → Ollama (`/v2/admin/settings?tab=ollama`) saves the internal server address, local text/vision models, optional encrypted gateway credential, text-provider selection, context window, inference deadline and keep-alive. Configuration uses `AppSetting.ai_ollama_v1` with environment fallback until saved; no schema migration. Public settings never return the key and remain readable to replace/clear a credential that cannot be decrypted. OpenAI and Claude remain supported. Existing AI workflow controls still govern photo assignment, comparison, historical examples and dedicated recognition; Ollama's model field there points to this shared setup.

The setup lists installed and loaded models, and explicitly downloads a selected local library model through Ollama's streaming pull API. The application does not install Docker, start services, or mount a Docker socket. A reachable private Ollama service with persistent storage is a prerequisite. Download progress is sanitized and bounded; missing completion is not success. A database lease serializes setup saves, diagnostics and downloads across app replicas, with expiry and token-bound release.

Separate checks verify server access, actual structured social text generation, four-image synthetic assignment with reference/history examples, and two-image synthetic comparison. Results include timestamps, duration and actionable sanitized connection/authentication/model/memory/timeout/format failures. A metadata check alone never marks inference working. Tests use generated colour cards and production result schemas, do not publish or mutate jobs/QA, and do not establish accuracy on real property photos. Dedicated property classifier training remains a separate service. Inference time is configurable from 30–300 seconds; browser assignment waits up to 360 seconds. Model downloads have a 30-minute bound. The server can require an encryption key only when storing a gateway credential; ordinary private Ollama does not require one.

**Resumed v2 verification (2026-09).** The September 13 continuation is recorded in `docs/qa/2026-09-09-ui-ux-execution-tracker.md` Wave 25. Implementation, automated checks, authenticated browser checks and actual device/provider delivery remain separate evidence. The local verification database uses disposable fixtures; the original application database is not used for test mutations.

**Command and Finance read reliability (2026-09).** Estate Command uses complete grouped unassigned counts instead of counting its 12-row preview. Critical query failures yield an unavailable/retry screen; the v2 caller requests strict dashboard metrics while classic callers retain their fallback compatibility. Schedule filters use UTC calendar keys and event windows use Sydney instants. Existing attention categories are linked explicitly, positive undisclosed work cannot show all-clear, and incomplete revenue is labelled as known charges with missing-rate guidance. Client Finance labels its latest-20-invoice and latest-50-completed-job scope; unbilled estimates are distinguished from payment requests and invoice totals from outstanding balances. Failed visibility or finance reads are unavailable rather than hidden access or zero balances. Invoice links retain server-scoped access and VA payment restrictions.

**Personal notification follow-up (2026-09).** Inbox recipients can flag personal action needed, resolve/clear that follow-up, archive and restore. A recipient/notification-scoped AppSetting uses optimistic revisions and transaction-backed audit records. These controls never change job resolution or provider delivery status. Filters state their loaded-history scope; stale responses cannot overwrite newer revisions. Impersonation cannot mutate follow-up or invoke the legacy notification-log deletion endpoint.

**Notification read and device state (2026-09).** Eligible recipient-owned sent PUSH notifications support explicit persistent read state and unread filtering; failed/provider delivery states cannot be overwritten by read actions, and impersonation cannot consume another account's unread state. Device setup separates browser support, permission, registration, own-account subscription and server configuration. Enable/repair/removal/local display testing require explicit user actions. A local display test is not evidence of provider delivery. Automatic permission requests and silent account rebinding were removed. Skipped lifecycle email delivery is no longer counted as sent; PUSH preferences use web consent and the existing calendar preference now persists.

**Cleaner calendar month correctness (2026-09).** Estate cleaner calendar loads active assignments within a validated selected Sydney month, groups dates in Sydney rather than UTC, and no longer truncates to the oldest 400 jobs. Month and agenda share that scope, with URL month navigation, Today and truthful read-error Retry. Rows label planned start/finish separately from the existing late-checkout and early-check-in constraints. This does not change assignment or timing policy; unified timing across other surfaces remains tracked work.

**VA booking context (2026-09).** Client layout banners display only confirmed VA client/team identity and explicitly label unavailable account context; impersonation changes invalidate that identity. Booking review shows the server-authorized actor/client plus property, service and full date. Its in-memory form is keyed to actor/client/team/property scope and available properties, availability requests reject stale results, and uncertain submission outcomes block immediate resend. Existing booking API errors can occur after a commit, so this does not replace pending server-side idempotency or durable scoped draft work.

**Booking retry protection (2026-09 update).** Estate booking now sends a UUID `Idempotency-Key` and explicitly retries the exact frozen body/key after uncertainty. The API scopes the deterministic lead ID to actor/key, compares a client/payload fingerprint under a transaction advisory lock, and returns an existing request without repeating writes or notifications. Both booking audit records commit with the lead. Notification rejection after commit returns success with a warning, not a false booking failure. Legacy callers without a key remain compatible but lack this retry guarantee; persisted draft/key recovery and crash-safe notification delivery are still pending. Receipts live on the lead and share its retention.

Booking availability additionally intersects the requested property with the authorized VA property scope. Booking request leads and contact fields use `portal.clientId`, while the acting user remains audit/request attribution; an assistant's unrelated or absent client link cannot determine lead ownership. Existing empty saved-list scope policy is preserved. Route tests mock provider calls; no live booking notification delivery is claimed.

**Booking review information (2026-09).** Private `GET /api/client/booking-review` authorizes booking access and exact property scope, returning validated guest-time defaults, an access-recorded boolean (never codes) and an existing-calculator estimate only when financial visibility/invoice-view grants allow it. Estate review labels this as a preliminary estimate, distinguishes guest times from a cleaning appointment, and explains that scheduling awaits review. Invalid prices/read failures are unavailable rather than zero, with retry and focus refresh. Catalogue service overviews are not contractual inclusions; detailed reservation/access prerequisites remain pending.

**Booking reload recovery (2026-09 update).** Estate booking persists validated drafts and exact request keys/payloads in same-tab sessionStorage, under a server-derived actor/client/team/property-scope digest. Editing dates revalidate; unresolved requests restore a frozen review and require explicit Retry; successful requests restore confirmation. A recovery-key write must succeed before POST, and a confirmed receipt must clear before another booking. Malformed/inaccessible storage fails explicitly without replacing the record. Cross-device/browser-close recovery and other forms remain pending.

**Portal record search (2026-09).** The shared Estate header exposes a keyboard-accessible search dialog for authorized pages and role-scoped records through private `GET /api/portal/search`. Search uses the active role, existing client/VA property/module grants and worker ownership rules, with bounded results, cancellation, explicit retry and identity cleanup. Admin/client invoice results open read-only `/v2/{admin,client}/finance/invoices/[id]` summaries with fresh server authorization; client actors cannot open draft, void or out-of-scope invoices. Missing billing-period dates are shown as unspecified. QA unclaimed work, worker invoices and broader historical Laundry search remain outside this slice; see the execution tracker for exact verification coverage.

The platform is a single **Next.js 14 App Router** application (`next@14.2.18`) backed by **Prisma + PostgreSQL**, operating on **Australia/Sydney** time for all scheduling. One codebase serves the public marketing site, all API routes, and six authenticated portals: admin (shared with ops-manager), cleaner, client, laundry driver, QA inspector, and maintenance.

Repo layout, in broad strokes:

- `app/` — routes. `app/api/**` holds every API handler; `app/admin`, `app/cleaner`, `app/client`, `app/laundry`, `app/qa`, `app/maintenance` are the **v1 (classic)** portals; `app/(public)` is the marketing site; `app/v2/**` is the **v2 "Estate"** experience with per-role subtrees (`app/v2/admin`, `app/v2/cleaner`, `app/v2/client`, `app/v2/laundry`, `app/v2/qa`, `app/v2/maintenance`), its own themed login (`app/v2/login`), onboarding, and theme layer (`app/v2/estate.css`, `app/v2/_theme-shell.tsx`).
- `components/v2/` — Estate UI, mirrored per role (`admin/`, `cleaner/`, `client/`, `laundry/`, `qa/`, `maintenance/`) plus `ui/` (the `EButton`/`ECard`/etc. primitive kit), `shared/`, `portal/`, and `public/`.
- `lib/` — all domain logic, one folder per concern (`lib/auth`, `lib/notifications`, `lib/ops`, `lib/gps`, `lib/finance`, `lib/quotes`, `lib/accountability`, `lib/laundry`, `lib/ical`, `lib/xero`, …). API routes are thin; behaviour lives here.
- `prisma/schema.prisma` + `prisma/migrations/` — the data model. `workers/` holds the pg-boss automation worker, which `npm run start` supervises as a sibling process unless a dedicated worker service is used (A7).
- `mobile-app/` — an Expo companion app (push tokens registered via `UserPushDevice`); `sneek-nextgen/` is a **separate, self-contained prototype workspace** (own `package.json`, own `prisma/`) for a future SaaS rebuild — it shares no code with the live app.

**v1 vs v2.** Both versions are fully routable forever; all new work targets v2 (v1 is legacy). The v2 pages generally call the *same* API routes as v1 — Estate is a re-skin plus workflow upgrades on a shared backend. Which version people *land in* is the "default look" system:

- `AppSettings.defaultPortalVersion` (`"v1" | "v2"`, default `"v1"` in `lib/settings.ts`) is the house default.
- A **personal override cookie** `sneek.portal-version` (90-day TTL) always beats the house default. Appending `?look=v2` (or `?look=v1`) to any URL sets it: `middleware.ts` catches the param, drops it, sets the cookie and redirects — plain-anchor switch links that work without JavaScript.
- `lib/portal-version.ts` is the pure, edge-safe half: `effectivePortalVersion(houseDefault, override)`, `portalRootIn()`, `versionOfPath()`. The critical design decision (documented in its header comment): **only portal roots are redirected** (`/admin ↔ /v2/admin` etc.). Deep links are never rewritten, so bookmarks/emailed links keep working and a page that exists in only one version stays reachable. `portalRootIn()` returns `null` when the path is already in the right version, which is what makes redirect loops impossible. The stored default lives via `lib/portal-version-store.ts` and rides to the edge middleware on the `/api/auth/validate-session` response (middleware cannot read Prisma).

### A2. Authentication & roles

**v2 first-login reliability (2026-09).** Login fields/submit/passkey controls remain disabled until React mounts, preventing native pre-hydration submissions. The extracted `lib/auth/credentials-sign-in.ts` detects NextAuth's explicit HTTP-200 CSRF rejection (`/api/auth/signin?csrf=true`), refreshes the token and retries once; other failed credentials are not retried. Missing or login-page redirect results are not treated as successful authentication. CSRF/2FA and account lockout enforcement remain unchanged. Regression tests and fresh cleaner/VA local browser checks cover this change.

Auth is **NextAuth with JWT sessions** (`lib/auth/auth-options.ts`), credentials-based (bcrypt password hashes on `User.passwordHash`). The `Role` enum (`prisma/schema.prisma`) is: `ADMIN`, `OPS_MANAGER`, `QA_INSPECTOR`, `CLEANER`, `CLIENT`, `LAUNDRY`, `MAINTENANCE`.

Role resolution deliberately does **not** trust the JWT alone — three layers re-check the database:

1. **Edge middleware** (`middleware.ts`) — the single choke point (matcher excludes only static assets). For page requests it calls `/api/auth/validate-session` (with the request cookies) to confirm the user is still active, pick up the *live* role, and learn `requiresPasswordReset` / `requiresOnboarding` / the house look. A fetch failure returns `"indeterminate"` and fails open to v1 rather than bouncing users. It then enforces the portal gates: `/admin` requires ADMIN or OPS_MANAGER (with `/admin/settings/pricebook` and `/admin/settings/pay-rates` further restricted to ADMIN); `/cleaner`, `/client`, `/laundry` require their exact role; `/maintenance` allows the worker plus admin/ops. For `/v2/*`, unauthenticated traffic is sent to `/v2/login` (the Estate-themed login — same NextAuth credentials/2FA, shared session cookie, different landing), `/v2` root goes to the role's home, and each `/v2/<portal>` subtree is checked against the owning role (admin/ops pass everywhere). The middleware also stamps security headers (CSP allowing Google Maps and blob: PDF previews, `X-Frame-Options: DENY`, etc.).
2. **`requireSession()` / `requireRole()`** (`lib/auth/session.ts`) — every API handler re-reads the `User` row, refuses inactive accounts, and overwrites `session.user.role` with the DB role. This is also where impersonation is layered in (A3).
3. **Login-time protections** — `lib/auth/login-lockout.ts` (attempt lockout), `lib/auth/temp-password.ts` / force-password-reset routing, invitation and registration OTP flows (`lib/auth/invitations.ts`, `registration-otp.ts`).

**2FA** (`lib/auth/twofactor.ts`) is per-user opt-in with two methods (`User.twoFactorMethod`): **TOTP** (otplib, secret in `User.totpSecret`, issuer "sNeek Ops") and **email one-time codes** (via `lib/auth/otp.ts`). Ten single-use **backup codes** are stored sha256-hashed in `User.twoFactorBackupCodes`. The flow's shape matters: the 2FA check runs *before* NextAuth `signIn` and mints a short-lived HMAC proof cookie (`sneek_2fa_ok`, 10 min); `authorize()` then only validates that cookie, so no second factor logic lives inside NextAuth. **Remember-device** issues a 30-day trusted-device token (`sneek_td`). Recovery lives at `/recover-2fa` (`lib/auth/recovery.ts`).

**WebAuthn / passkeys** exist too (`lib/auth/webauthn.ts`, `@simplewebauthn/server`): platform-biometric sign-in with challenges stashed in short-lived signed httpOnly cookies (`sneek-wa-reg` / `sneek-wa-auth`, 5-min TTL, single-use), rpID derived from the request hostname so localhost and production both verify.

### A3. Impersonation — admin "Test as"

An admin can view the app as any non-admin user. The design (thoroughly documented in `lib/auth/impersonation.ts`) rests on **not touching the real session**: because both middleware and `requireSession()` re-read the DB role, spoofing the JWT wouldn't even work. Instead a second, **NextAuth-signed cookie** `sneek.test-as` carries an `ImpersonationTicket` (`actorId`, `targetId`, `targetRole`, `mode`, `startedAt`) with its own **1-hour TTL**. Consequences: sign-out/expiry/deactivation behave normally, dropping the cookie is an instant complete revert, and the audit trail always names the real admin.

- **Edge half** (`lib/auth/impersonation.ts`, importable from middleware): verifies signature and shape; `isReadOnlySafeMethod()` defines GET/HEAD/OPTIONS as safe. `middleware.ts` enforces **READ_ONLY mode** at the choke point: any non-safe method while a READ_ONLY ticket is present is refused with a 403 (`IMPERSONATION_READ_ONLY`) — except the `/api/admin/impersonate` endpoints themselves, or you could never exit. READ_ONLY is the default; FULL exists for end-to-end testing, is chosen explicitly, and is recorded in the audit row.
- **Server half** (`lib/auth/impersonation-server.ts`): a valid signature is *not* sufficient authority. On every request `resolveImpersonation()` re-checks live rows: the ticket must belong to this session (a leaked cookie is useless elsewhere), the actor must still be an **active ADMIN** (OPS_MANAGER never qualifies; demotion revokes instantly), and the target must exist, be active, and **not be an admin** — so impersonation can only move sideways or down, never escalate.
- `requireSession()` swaps `session.user` for the target (so every downstream query "just works") and attaches `session.impersonation` for the warning banner. **`requireRealSession()`** ignores impersonation entirely and is used wherever the human at the keyboard must be attributed — including the impersonate endpoints themselves (otherwise an impersonated CLEANER session could never pass the ADMIN check needed to stop).
- **Unlock gate (2026-07).** Being an admin is no longer enough: the Test-as console is password/PIN-locked. `lib/auth/test-as-unlock.ts` mints a **15-minute HMAC proof cookie** `sneek.test-as-unlock` (`<userId>.<expiryMs>.<hmac>`, same shape as the 2FA proof) after the admin re-enters their credentials via `POST /api/admin/test-as/unlock` (which uses the existing `verifySensitiveAction` bcrypt check and is rate-limited to **5 attempts per 15 minutes** per admin). Crucially it is a *cookie*, not sessionStorage, so `POST /api/admin/impersonate` (`app/api/admin/impersonate/route.ts`) can enforce it server-side — a direct POST without a fresh unlock gets a 403 `TEST_AS_LOCKED` regardless of what the browser claims. Start and stop are both audited (`IMPERSONATE_START` written *before* the cookie is issued, so a crash can't produce an unrecorded impersonation; `IMPERSONATE_STOP` records duration).

### A3b. Access and permissions (2026-11 redesign)

Settings › Access and permissions (`/v2/admin/settings/access`; the old `?tab=roles` URL redirects; settings catalog key `roles` now opens it). One screen for office access: people grouped as Administrators, Operations managers, Office assistants (OPS_MANAGER logins whose preset has `audience: "assistant"`; no new role) and Other staff with office access (OPS_MANAGER held as an extra role); own-portal counts; client assistant teams. Tabs: By person, By area, Presets, Client assistants, History.

**Model (extends `ops_feature_permissions_v1`; no schema change).** Area levels are `off | read | manage | full`. A person's level = their override ?? their preset's level ?? Off; no assignment = every area at Manage (the compatibility default). **Full** routes exactly like Manage (`resolveOpsLevels` collapses it, so middleware, navigation and `canUseOpsPath` are unchanged) and adds the area's sensitive capabilities. Fine-grained capabilities live in ONE catalog, `lib/rbac/capabilities.ts`, each with an area, a tier and its enforcement point:
- `view` comes with View (shown only); `manage` comes with Manage and can be switched off per person or preset (`capabilities: { key: false }`; `true` never widens past the level);
- `full` is one of the eight sensitive grants: explicit grant (person, then custom preset) ?? area at Full; still verified with the person's own PIN/password (`verifySensitiveAction`), also at Full;
- `grant` (owner decision 2026-11-12) is an administrator capability: password reset, two-step reset, Test as, Edit access and permissions. Off for every non-administrator until an ADMIN grants it to that person (never through a preset, schema-refused). Sensitive, PIN-protected.
The single evaluator is `lib/rbac/access-evaluator.ts` (pure); `lib/rbac/can.ts` (`can(session, key)`, `canSubject`, `requireCapability`) reads the live policy on every call (no cache: a saved change applies to the person's next request). ADMIN (primary or extra) answers true without reading a policy; impersonated sessions are the target's. Enforcement points: invoice PATCH (approve/send/record payment/void), bulk, send and credit-note routes (`lib/rbac/invoice-capabilities.ts`, `capability-http.ts`); job cancel; message composer send; the office-send gate (`officeSendDecision`: supplier chase, guest message, client notice, client delay, clock pause, account emails); urgent-stock writes (`urgentOfficeAllowed`); `verifySensitiveAction` for the eight sensitive actions. `tests/lib/access-parity.test.ts` proves defaults reproduce the earlier rules for every capability and account kind.

**Delegated administrator capabilities** (`lib/rbac/delegated-admin.ts`, `delegated-edit.ts`). Owner-only routes they open are listed in `ownerRouteCapability` (`ops-catalog.ts`); `enforceOpsRequest` and middleware (`opsGrants` from validate-session; always `[]` while impersonating) allow those paths only with the matching grant, and the route re-checks. Guardrails on the server: never while impersonating; never on an administrator, on anyone whose access (any level or capability) exceeds the actor's, or on oneself; a delegated editor cannot change presets, administrator capabilities, their own access, or give anyone more than they hold. Test as is re-checked on every request in `resolveImpersonation`. Every use is audited against the actual actor with `actorRole`. Still administrator only: granting these capabilities, role changes to/from ADMIN, security settings, integration credentials, settings audit/restore, Insights email recipients (reasons in `ADMIN_ONLY_ACTIONS`). Last-administrator safeguards are unchanged (role changes stay in Accounts).

**People page tabs (2026-11-12).** `ops-catalog.ts` `PEOPLE_TAB_FEATURES`: the page `/v2/admin/accounts?tab=clients` belongs to the clients pack and `?tab=cleaners` to the workforce pack; every other tab and the bare page stay with Staff accounts; `opsNavigationHref` sends a clients- or workforce-only manager to their tab. API paths are never steered by `tab` (`/api/admin/clients/*` = clients, `/api/admin/users` = accounts). The page (`app/v2/admin/accounts/page.tsx`) gates tabs with `allowedPeopleTabs({clients, accounts, workforce})` and skips every Staff-accounts read (birthdays, role counts, invitations, suppliers, active-client list, cleaner figures unless Cleaners is open) without that pack. A workforce-only manager's Cleaners list reads `GET /api/admin/people/cleaners` (and `/export`), mapped to the workforce pack, which forces the cleaners tab and computes counts and summary from cleaner rows only; rows open the cleaner's workforce page instead of the Staff accounts panel. `/v2/admin/clients`, `/cleaners` and `/workforce` redirect to the People tab for every viewer whose pack opens it; their separate nav entries are removed (Coaching kept).

**Saving.** `PUT /api/admin/ops-permissions` `{ policy, security }`: live guard, delegated check, revision check (409 returns `current`; the screen lists both versions and offers "put my changes on top" or "load theirs"), PIN/password, then `saveOpsPolicy` (advisory lock, revision guard, refuses any change to the actor's own entry or preset, audit row `OPS_PERMISSIONS_UPDATED` with `before`, `after` and `after.audit = { actorId, actorRole, impersonatedBy, changes }`). History is derived from those rows (`lib/rbac/access-diff.ts`), shown in the organisation timezone; people not changed for 90 days are flagged "Review". Client assistant teams are edited through the existing `GET /api/admin/va-invites` and `PATCH /api/admin/va-teams/[id]` (`lib/va/teams.ts`), with the never-delegable money rule shown.

### A4. Settings system

**Return navigation and operations access (2026-10).** Root portal navigation
remembers temporary view queries, tabs, list filters and resumable draft steps per
browser tab, identity/role and record. Returning overrides saved defaults; explicit
deep links win. Full refresh resets transient view state while saved Jobs defaults
and draft/evidence recovery remain intact. Jobs uses client navigation for Manage;
new onboarding surveys move to their saved draft URL. QA remembers its guided
step alongside its local draft and returns to step one if that draft is absent.
Classic and Estate client Jobs now scope their views and calendar months to this identity/tab,
replacing the old unscoped persistent preference. This covers the root app,
not the separate mobile/nextgen applications.

Settings → Access → Roles now manages each operations manager's permission pack
and per-feature Off / View only / Manage overrides. Built-in packs preserve current
access, provide an observer/coordinator level, or start with all features off;
admins can create reusable custom packs. `ops_feature_permissions_v1` is a
revisioned AppSetting, with transactional conflict protection and audit entries.
Fresh server checks supplement existing role and entity guards. Manage does not
grant admin-only operations; ADMIN accounts remain outside these restrictions.
Impersonation uses the effective manager, and retained routes preserve their
selected identity. Navigation and record search reflect permitted features.

Feature ownership and aliases are centralized in `lib/rbac/ops-catalog.ts`;
unmapped admin routes fail closed. Permissions describe whole workspaces, not
field-level redaction: Jobs includes job billing, Properties includes its rate
cards, and Dashboard/Reports retain their cross-feature summaries. Files/media have a separate feature gate, with job/property preview checks. Map,
account-self-service and worker-ownership utilities retain existing guards.
A missing initial policy preserves existing access; malformed stored policy fails
closed. No schema migration is required. Standards and verification details:
`docs/style/operational-ui.md` and `docs/qa/recent-changes-review-2026-10-09.md`.

**Settings navigation (2026-10).** The Estate settings page now uses a searchable
category directory, one horizontal category row and current-category links on
larger screens, and a grouped native selector on phones. The role-filtered catalog
is `components/v2/admin/settings/settings-catalog.ts`; existing `?tab=` keys are
preserved. Public holiday rates are part of **Money**, and property-form editing is
part of **Operations**. Both old holiday-rate routes and the Estate property-form
route redirect into the same settings shell; holiday job context is preserved.
Existing forms, persistence and server permissions remain authoritative (including
OPS_MANAGER access to the property-form editor). Job holiday review appears in
**Job → Money** for ADMIN, with a contextual return link. Classic settings links
to the same rate editor from its Finance category. Classic job navigation consumes
the server-derived `capabilities.reviewHolidayRates` field (effective role), not
the primary role cached in the browser session. The overview uses the settings
already loaded by the page; navigation search opts out of saved-text suggestions.

**Optional bag tracking placement (2026-10).** The global individual-bag log now
lives only in Laundry → Tracking, with task-scoped cleaner-job and admin-investigation
entries retained. Queue and Runs use their existing run actions. The UI explains
that the optional labelled-bag observations help trace missing bags and do not
advance run status or prove recipient acceptance. ADMIN/LAUNDRY visibility in the
tracking page matches the existing custody service; OPS_MANAGER gets no unusable
bag editor. Laundry settings remain configuration, not an event-recording surface.

All tunable behaviour lives in one JSON blob per key in the `AppSetting` table (`key` / `value Json`), read through **`lib/settings.ts`** (~72 KB — the platform's control panel). The pattern is uniform: every settings group has a **TypeScript interface**, a **default**, and a **sanitizer** that coerces whatever is stored back into shape, so a partial or legacy row can never crash a read. `getAppSettings()` merges DB over defaults; admin pages under `/v2/admin/settings/**` write back.

Notable groups on the `AppSettings` interface:

**Browser and installed-app branding (2026-10).** The saved light-background Company & brand logo now supplies `/favicon.ico`, `/icon`, the versioned `/icons/{size}` PNG routes and a dynamic `/manifest.json`. Root metadata supplies a dedicated 180px Apple touch icon; Android receives 192/512px regular icons plus a separate 512px maskable icon with safe padding. The manifest identity/start URL remains `/`, and its name follows the company name. Branding changes version the icon URLs. Manifest/icon routes bypass service-worker runtime caching; generated icons revalidate after five minutes. Failed asset reads return the bundled logo without caching the failure. Existing phone shortcuts may require removal and re-addition. No migration or new branding field is needed.

- **Identity/branding**: `companyName`, `companyPhone`, `logoUrl` / `logoDarkBgUrl` / `reportLogoUrl`, `timezone`, `portalTheme`, `websiteContent` (the whole public site's copy), `defaultPortalVersion` (A1).
- **`accountability`** — the cleaner quality system: `scoring` (minor/major/critical deductions, score floor, band thresholds), `bonuses` (quality-streak and monthly-ranking amounts and eligibility minima), `rectification` (time-banded deduction amounts, manager-review threshold, rework-offer TTL), admin-defined `issueCategories`, pattern-detection windows, `requireJobStartConfirmation`, `selfInspectionBlocksSubmit`.
- **`autoClockOut`** — `enabled`, `stopAtEstimatedDuration` (legacy cut-off, default false — the timer runs past the estimate), `graceMinutes`, `fallbackAtMidnight`, `maxJobLengthHours`, and **`geofenceExit` (2026-07, default true)** — the kill-switch for GPS departure auto clock-out (A8).
- **`finalCheckup` (2026-07)** — the pre-submit acknowledgement dialog for cleaners: `enabled` + admin-authored `items[]` (`FinalCheckupItem`: title, detail, `referenceImageKeys` uploaded via `/api/uploads/direct` folder `final-checkup`, and `appliesTo` job types).
- **`qaPay` (2026-07)** — global defaults for what a QA inspection pays: `defaultMode` (`FIXED | HOURLY | NONE`), `defaultFixedAmount`, `defaultHourlyRate`, `defaultHoursPerInspection`. Resolution order is assignment override (`QaAssignment.payMode/payAmount/...`) → inspector's `User.hourlyRate` → these settings (`lib/finance/qa-pay.ts`).
- **Visibility toggles** — `clientPortalVisibility` (some two dozen flags controlling what clients can see/do, including **`showLiveProgress` (2026-07, ships OFF)** — live mid-clean checklist progress on the client job page), `cleanerPortalVisibility`, `laundryPortalVisibility`.
- **`laundryOperations`** — pickup cutoff/default times, `maxOutdoorDays`, fast-return behaviour when no next clean exists.
- **Automation groups** — `sla`, `caseAutomation` (severity-gated auto case opening with dedupe and auto-resolve), `recurringJobs`, `autoAssign`, `routeOptimization`, `qaAutomation`, `scheduledNotifications`, `notificationDefaults`, `emailAutomation`, `notificationAudienceControls`, `evidenceStamp` (date/time format burned into photos), `pricing` (cleaner cost / rack rate / margin floor), `pricingVariables`, `invoicing` (bank details, ABN, terms), `emailTemplates` / `notificationTemplates`.

**Integrations are settings-first with env fallback.** Credentials for every external service live in the `AppSetting` row `integrationCredentials`, edited at `app/api/admin/integrations/credentials/route.ts`: Resend, Twilio, Cellcast, S3/R2, Stripe, Square, PayPal, Xero, Google Maps, VAPID web-push keys, bootstrap admin. Consumers (e.g. `lib/s3.ts`, `lib/xero/client.ts`, `lib/notifications/web-push.ts`, `lib/maps/server-key.ts`) prefer the DB value and fall back to the corresponding env var, so a fresh deployment works from env alone and the UI can take over later. Sensitive keys are never returned unmasked (masked round-trip: bullet-containing values mean "unchanged"), changing or *clearing* any `SENSITIVE_KEYS` entry requires the admin to **re-enter their account password**, changes are audited with secrets redacted, and the truly system-critical values (`DATABASE_URL`, `NEXTAUTH_SECRET`) are env-only and shown read-only.

### A5. Notifications

**Cleaner continuity and approval reliability (2026-09).** The cleaner shell shows an in-flow active-job property/status/resume strip away from its own workspace. Its scoped probe fails closed with Retry and disables background GPS on failed verification. GPS queued fixes carry local cleaner/impersonation scope, never sent in API payloads; unowned legacy and other-user records are not transmitted. Stale callbacks are ignored, flushes serialize and cleanup aborts requests. Existing PAUSED tracking policy remains unchanged. Client approvals now show unavailable/stale states without false All clear, preserve notes on transient failures and block overlapping submissions. All six approval JSON-store writers lock inside one ReadCommitted PostgreSQL transaction before reading; automatic history eviction is removed. Displayed-version validation and real-device GPS checks remain separate gates.

**Navigation refresh and groups (2026-09).** Show all pages clears navigation search and expands authorized groups while preserving favorites; it remembers that expansion per user/portal. Cleaner, QA, Laundry and Maintenance attention badges use a shared session-scoped poll with abort/serialization, validated counts and notification-event refresh. Transient failures retain current-user counts; denied access clears them. Cleaner/QA maintenance navigation resets across user, role and impersonation changes and hides on failed assignment refresh. Named sidebar groups remember collapse per user/portal; search temporarily reveals matches and arriving at an active group reveals its destination. Client home now distinguishes failed queries from valid zero/empty results with per-section unavailable states and Retry, preserving successful sections and VA visibility restrictions.

**Recent notification inbox (2026-09).** The shared v2 portal header exposes a searchable, read-only panel of the latest up to 200 personal PUSH records through `/api/notifications/log`. It supports loading, refresh, Retry and explicit empty states; links use the server's role/version resolution. The history response is private/no-store and database failures return a generic retryable error. No read/unread status or destructive clear action is implied. Requests are serialized and aborted on closure; user/role/impersonation changes reset the panel, and 401/403 clears cached rows. The global live listener also resets subscriptions and bounded deduplication on identity changes and ignores stale priming/events. Browser evidence, provider/device gaps and the remaining notification lifecycle are recorded in the execution tracker.

**Client/VA navigation trust (2026-09).** Attention-count responses enforce VA grants and property scope, omit approval/quote counts for assistants, and use finance's all-lines-in-scope rule for restricted invoices. Database failures return 503 rather than successful zeros. The client hook serializes refreshes, validates data, binds state to session identity, clears stale grants on errors and all navigation on access denial, and exposes Retry. Before identity resolves no client-only links are offered. Laundry home now separates failed queue/route/property lookups from empty results with compact unavailable/retry states; scheduled-date modeling is unchanged.

**Notification stream lifecycle (2026-09).** SSE polls are serialized and revalidate the active user, role and impersonation before querying and before releasing results. Abort/cancel closes timers; connections are capped at 60 seconds to refresh request cookies. Ready and notification events carry user-bound cursor IDs; reconnects use validated Last-Event-ID values and a bounded 10-minute replay window. A valid older cursor is clamped to that window, so a quiet connection does not discard recent reconnect-gap events. Device delivery and ordinary browser SSE behavior remain separate verification gates from the mocked route tests.

**Agent-assisted UI/UX wave 2 (2026-09).** Estate navigation favorites are stored per signed-in user and portal on the current browser, intersected with currently allowed navigation, and moved into one Favorites group without duplicate links. Pin/unpin never changes mobile tabs. QA dashboard metrics now distinguish query failure from zero, expose Retry, use Sydney calendar-day UTC boundaries across DST, and label the rework count as team-wide while retaining inspector ownership filters. Notification-stream lifecycle hardening and verification are recorded in the execution tracker; real device delivery is a separate release gate.

**v2 reliability foundation (2026-09).** Notification feed/log and stream links resolve the viewer's portal-version preference and use role-appropriate job destinations for client, VA, cleaner and QA. Laundry and maintenance fall back to their own portal, never admin. The template notification engine now records its unsupported PUSH transport as FAILED rather than claiming a console log was delivery; the separate existing device-delivery pipeline remains unchanged. Browser permission is requested only through the existing user-initiated WebPushSubscriber flow, not automatically by LiveNotifications.

**Shared navigation and maintenance dashboard (2026-09).** Estate portals use an accessible Radix mobile drawer with Escape/focus restoration, named close control and reduced-motion-aware entrance. Sidebar search filters only supplied role-visible destinations. Mobile tab destinations are configured independently in `lib/navigation/mobile-tabs.ts` and intersected with authorized navigation, preserving badges without importing unauthorized links. The Maintenance home total uses an independent count rather than its 20-row preview; missing query results are shown as unavailable with Retry, and its date boundaries use the shared Sydney calendar helpers. This does not change maintenance worker visibility policy. Implementation and verification are tracked in `docs/qa/2026-09-09-ui-ux-execution-tracker.md`.

The in-app feed is the `Notification` model (`userId`, `jobId`, `channel` PUSH/EMAIL/SMS, subject/body, status, plus `externalId`/`deliveryStatus` for provider webhook matching; indexed on `(userId, channel, createdAt)` because the SSE stream polls it per connected user).

The **single chokepoint** is `deliverNotificationToRecipients()` in `lib/notifications/delivery.ts`. For each (deduped) recipient it resolves three gates per channel — the per-user **category preference** (`lib/notifications/preferences.ts`; categories are `account`, `jobs`, `laundry`, `cases`, `reports`, `quotes`, `shopping`, `billing`, `approvals`, `ical`), the **audience-level controls** (`lib/notifications/audience-controls.ts`: global + per-audience email/sms/push switches that override user opt-ins), and for email the **email-automation kind switch** (`lib/notifications/email-kinds.ts`; each send carries an `EmailAutoKind` so one toggle no longer silences the whole pipeline). Then:

- **Web**: writes the feed row *and* fires real browser **Web Push** (`lib/notifications/web-push.ts`, VAPID via `web-push`; keys resolved settings-first — `getServerVapidPublicKey()` reads `integrationCredentials` then env — and served to the browser via `/api/public/push-config`; no-ops gracefully when unconfigured). Deep links fall back to a role-appropriate href from `lib/notifications/feed.ts`.
- **Mobile push**: the separate Expo channel (`lib/notifications/mobile-push.ts`) sends to `UserPushDevice` tokens through the Expo push API.
- **Email**: `lib/notifications/email.ts` sends through **Resend**, wrapping non-full-document HTML in the branded shell (`wrapEmailHtml` from `lib/email-templates.ts`) and absolutizing inline links. A **suppression list** (`lib/email/suppression.ts`: hard/soft bounces, complaints, unsubscribes) blocks marketing sends; `transactional: true` (password reset, OTP, invoices) bypasses it, and `critical: true` additionally bypasses audience gating so auth emails can never be silenced. Every send is logged back as a Notification row with the Resend message id.
- **SMS**: `lib/notifications/sms.ts` supports **Twilio or Cellcast** (chosen by `AppSettings.smsProvider`), normalizes AU numbers (04… → +61…), and clamps bodies to 459 chars (~3 GSM segments) to bound per-message billing.

**The email gate, narrowest-last (2026-08).** `sendEmailDetailed` now applies four decisions in order, each narrower than the one before: the **master** switch, the **kind** switch, a **per-audience override of a kind** (`EmailAutomationSettings.audienceKinds` — "cleaners don't need inventory mail" without also silencing the ops manager who does), and finally the **person's own preference** (`UserEmailPreference` rows, plus `User.allEmailOff` for "stop emailing me entirely" — one flag rather than a row per kind, so it keeps working when new kinds are added later). Every link fails **open**: a missing map, audience, kind or preference row means allowed, so a switch can only ever remove mail someone chose to remove, never withhold it by omission. `critical: true` (auth, OTP, 2FA) bypasses all of it, or a person could lock themselves out of their own account by ticking a box. The audience/preference checks run **per recipient**, so a multi-recipient send now drops only the people who opted out instead of all-or-nothing. A caller-supplied `audience` still skips the user lookup, but only for a single recipient on a **kindless** send — otherwise the row is needed to answer the per-person question. Migration: `20260803000000_email_preferences`. Regression: `tests/lib/email-audience-kinds.test.ts`.

Alongside that, automatic sends that carried **no `kind`** were backfilled — job assignment and bulk-assign digests, pay-adjustment decisions and requests, workforce/document emails, QA client invites, lost-and-found and client-approval alerts, public quote responses. Those sends were unswitchable, which is why the `pay_adjustment` and `workforce_update` toggles existed in the admin UI while controlling nothing. Admin-*clicked* sends (quote send, invoice send, report share, PO email, test notification, invitations, OTP) stay deliberately kindless: they are a person pressing send, not the system deciding to.

**Who decides, and who is told (2026-08, wave 4 part 2).** The gate above is now editable rather than API-only: the comms control center carries an audience x kind grid (a cell only ever stores `false`, so unticking removes mail someone chose to remove and a kind added later arrives allowed for every group), and a per-person section with select-all, "turn this type off/on for the selected people" and "all email off" — writing `UserEmailPreference` rows and `User.allEmailOff` through `POST /api/admin/users/email-preferences/bulk`. That route only touches the kinds it is given, so setting one preference cannot silently reset the others, and an "allowed" choice deletes the row rather than storing `true`. The settings PATCH schema had to learn `audienceKinds` explicitly — zod strips unknown keys, so without it the grid would have appeared to save and come back empty.

**Three changes that used to happen in silence (2026-08).** `job_task_added`: `syncAdminJobTasks` added work to someone's job and notified nobody, so a cleaner who had already read their brief arrived without the extra task — it now tells the job's current cleaners about NEWLY created tasks only (renaming one is not worth a 6am push), skipping finished jobs, and a notification failure can never undo the tasks that saved. `special_note_changed`: the admin job PATCH tested the *payload* (`if (body.internalNotes ?? "").trim()`), so re-saving an unchanged job announced a change that hadn't happened while CLEARING a note registered nothing at all — the case that matters most, since the cleaner keeps following a withdrawn instruction. It now diffs the old and new note and puts the text in the summary. `timing_rule_changed`: a job edit that is ONLY the turnaround rules, or ONLY the note, now carries its own kind, while a mixed edit stays on the general job-update kind so silencing note changes can never silence a reschedule.

**Reassignment is now a decision, not a side effect (2026-08).** Replacing a job's primary cleaner used to fire the `CLEANER_ASSIGNED` "meet your cleaner" email again — stating a change as though it were the original news, before anyone had decided whether to explain it. A new `CLEANER_REASSIGNED` stage leads with the change, names the replacement, carries an optional reason and reassures that the time and access arrangements are unchanged. The assign route no longer auto-sends in that case: it returns the rendered draft, and the shared assign modal (used by the job page, the board and the calendar alike) opens a confirm dialog with an editable subject and body plus a plain "Don't send". `sendLifecycleEmail` gained an `override`, because offering an edit and then sending the template anyway would be worse than never offering it. A FIRST assignment is uncontroversial and still sends automatically.

**Pay-adjustment notifications (2026-07)** — `lib/notifications/pay-adjustments.ts` is a dedicated module for money changes: a `CleanerPayAdjustment` moving to APPROVED (or an approved one being edited/reversed), and the QA-rework transfer path that writes an already-approved deduction against the cleaner and credit to the inspector, now notify both parties about the *money* through the full chokepoint (web push + email + SMS, preference-aware). The helpers fire only on a real state transition, so retried requests cannot double-notify. (Legacy note: two route-local `createRoleNotifications()` helpers still exist in the laundry status routes, e.g. `app/api/laundry/[taskId]/status/route.ts`, fanning feed rows out to whole roles — pre-chokepoint code that newer work no longer copies.)

### A6. Uploads & media

**Cleaner multi-device evidence and recovery (2026-10).** A cleaner may upload from multiple authenticated devices on the same account and assigned job. The Estate workspace reads the shared draft receipts every 10 seconds while visible and on focus, reconciling uploaded media, moves and removal tombstones without replacing unsaved answers. Pending local edits suppress a stale read; obsolete reads are aborted. This is confirmed-upload synchronization, not replication of device-local pending files or collaborative form-answer editing. Account/job authorization and original capture ownership remain enforced.

Bulk thumbnails select; the separate Preview action opens an in-page image/video dialog. Manual multi-photo assignment reads one receipt snapshot and processes up to three version-checked moves concurrently, retaining confirmed successes and identifying failures. An owned older-form receipt can be explicitly moved after current form/destination/capacity validation; the original capture context is retained and the rebind audited. Removing an owned older-form reference records a tombstone without deleting the original. Other account/job contexts retain explicit reference-discard or office review requirements. Office Draft evidence review uses the shared media popup.


**Cleaner video compatibility and failed-upload removal (2026-09).** If browser video compression fails, originals up to 150 MiB can upload through the existing multipart flow; cancellation never triggers a fallback, and larger failures retain their originals for recovery. Common video extensions supply a video content type when phones omit it. Failed uploads now have an explicit removal action in the field and device recovery panel. Removal requires a server acknowledgement and records a detached capture even when no upload allocation exists, preventing delayed attachment requests from restoring it. Originals remain on the device until explicitly cleared; remote objects are not deleted. Required evidence fields still need replacement evidence before submission.

Object storage is **S3-compatible (Cloudflare R2)** via `lib/s3.ts` — an `aws-sdk` v2 client, settings-first (A4) with a 60-second credential cache, plus `publicUrl()` (CDN base from `S3_PUBLIC_BASE_URL`) and presigned GET/PUT helpers.

**Uploads died because the page reloaded itself (2026-08).** `next.config.mjs` had `reloadOnOnline: true` — a `@ducanh2912/next-pwa` option (its default) that injects `addEventListener("online", () => location.reload())` into the service-worker register script. Any momentary connectivity blip therefore hard-reloaded the page, and an upload is the longest, heaviest network operation in the app, so it was the thing most likely to be in flight when that happened. The browser aborted the POST and the server logged `Error: aborted … ECONNRESET` from `abortIncoming` — a body that started arriving and then stopped. It presented as "I pick a photo, press OK, the page refreshes and nothing uploads", looked account-specific because it tracks the person's *network* rather than their identity, and never reproduced locally because the service worker is disabled in development. Now `false`: reloading on reconnect discards whatever the user was in the middle of, and refreshing data after a genuine absence is `components/shared/return-sync.tsx`'s job — which itself now excludes file-dialog round trips, since opening a picker hides the tab and used to be mistaken for the user leaving.

**ROOT CAUSE FOUND — the gallery path still decoded at full resolution (2026-08).** The `decodeScaled` fix below covered only `stampImage` (camera shots). Gallery picks — the main path in cleaning-report submission — went through `compressImage` → `browser-image-compression`, which decodes the photo at FULL resolution in a worker before shrinking it: 190–430 MB of RGBA for a modern 48–108 MP phone photo, allocated the instant the picker returns. Android answers by killing the tab, which reloads — the "quick flash, nothing uploaded" report. This also explains every observed asymmetry: cleaners only (only evidence flows compress client-side), the laundry portal fine on the same phone (no image processing), unreproducible on desktop (memory headroom). Fixed at the chokepoint: `compressImage` now uses `downscaleImage` (new export in `stamp.ts`, same `createImageBitmap`-scaled decode as `stampImage`, peak memory ≈ the 2400 px working bitmap ≈ 23 MB) with the library kept only as a fallback for files the scaled decoder refuses. Every caller — v1 + v2 cleaner, QA, upload-dropzone — is covered with no call-site changes.

**Decode at the target size, never at full resolution (2026-08).** `stampImage` (`lib/uploads/stamp.ts`) used to load the photo through `new Image()`, which materialises the entire bitmap before anything is drawn — a 48MP phone photo is roughly 190MB of RGBA, allocated the instant the file picker returns. Desktops absorbed it; phones did not. iOS Safari and Android Chrome answer that spike by discarding the tab and reloading, which cleaners experienced as "I picked a photo, the page went back to the start, and nothing uploaded" — on phones and the installed app only, and on *every* upload in the portal, because every evidence photo goes through this function. It now decodes with `createImageBitmap(file, { resizeWidth, resizeHeight, resizeQuality })` so the browser produces the reduced image directly, and releases the source (`bitmap.close()`) as soon as it is drawn rather than holding it for the rest of the stamp. Browsers without `createImageBitmap`, and files it rejects (some HEIC paths), fall back to the previous element decode.

**Upload watchdog — removed (2026-08-04).** While the "picking a photo refreshes the page" bug was open, a temporary diagnostic (`components/shared/upload-watchdog.tsx` + `POST /api/debug/upload-watchdog`) instrumented every teardown shape an upload can die by — in-flight beacons, file-pick and picker-open sessionStorage crumbs read on the next load, and app-remount reports. It was deleted once the root cause was confirmed fixed (the workspace-refresh unmount; see the Change Log). Two portable lessons survive it: `location.reload` is an unforgeable, non-configurable property (`defineProperty` throws, so no wrapper can ever capture the caller's stack), and page-level `window.fetch` instrumentation must install once at module scope — two wrappers that each restore "the original" on unmount clobber each other, and a diagnostic that can uninstall itself reports nothing while looking exactly like proof that nothing happened.

The workhorse is **`POST /api/uploads/direct`** (`app/api/uploads/direct/route.ts`): a server-side upload that accepts multipart form data, validates folder + content type (`lib/uploads/validate.ts`), enforces 20 MB for images and ~150 MB for videos, transcodes/compresses videos to MP4 on the server (`lib/media/video-compression.ts`, keeping the original when compression doesn't help), and puts the object under `<folder>/<userId>/<uuid>.<ext>`. **Why server-side rather than presigned browser PUTs**: the presign path (`/api/uploads/presign`) *silently did nothing in production* — the bucket has no CORS configuration for browser PUTs, so uploads appeared to succeed and dropped the bytes. That failure and the switch to the direct route are recorded in comments at each former call site (e.g. `components/v2/client/quote-wizard.tsx`, `components/v2/admin/forms/builder/theme-editor.tsx`, `components/forms/reference-media-editor.tsx`). The presign and multipart routes still exist for legacy/large-file paths (`lib/uploads/multipart-client.ts`).

Reads go through **`GET /api/uploads/access`** (`app/api/uploads/access/route.ts`): session-checked, key-validated (no `..`), role-gated, returning a time-limited presigned GET — so evidence is never publicly enumerable. Cleaner evidence photos are **stamped client-side before upload** (`lib/uploads/stamp.ts`: timestamp, weekday, logo burned into the image per `AppSettings.evidenceStamp`), and form evidence is persisted as **`SubmissionMedia`** rows (per submission + field: `s3Key`, `url`, optional `originalS3Key` and `annotatedS3Key`/`annotationData` so annotation never destroys the original).

**The unauthenticated failure log (2026-08).** `POST /api/admin/system/uploads/log-failure` records client-side upload failures and is deliberately **unauthenticated** — an upload can fail before or independently of a session (an expired cookie, a guest form), and capturing exactly those is the point — which made it the only unauthenticated write handler on the surface, reachable from the internet. It accepted unbounded strings and a `z.record(z.any())` `context`, so a single request could push megabytes of arbitrarily nested JSON into a row, and `await req.json()` sat outside any try, so a malformed body rejected out of the handler as an unhandled 500. It now caps every field, restricts `context` to scalars under both a key-count and a serialised-size limit, rate-limits per IP (`lib/security/rate-limit.ts`), and answers 400 on an unparseable body. The rate limit is defence-in-depth only and is documented as such at the call site: `getClientIp` trusts the first `x-forwarded-for` hop, which a caller can spoof, and the limiter is per-process rather than shared — the field caps are the real bound on what any one request can cost.

### A7. Scheduler

Background automation is registered in **`workers/boss.ts`**, a dedicated pg-boss process with explicit Australia/Sydney schedules. `lib/ops/web-scheduler.ts` is an inert compatibility shim; page renders and Jobs navigation never schedule work. Stored schedule rows survive stopped workers and cannot prove liveness. (2026-10)

**Automation in a single container (2026-11).** Between 4 October and this change a deployment that started only the web server ran no automation at all: the in-web fallback had been made inert and nothing replaced it, so iCal sync, reminders, notification dispatch, recurring jobs, sweeps and campaigns stopped together. `npm run start` (and therefore the Dockerfile `CMD` and a platform start command of `npm run start`) now also launches `scripts/embedded-worker.cjs`, a small supervisor that runs the same `workers/boss.ts` worker as a **separate sibling process**. Requests still never schedule work, and a worker crash or hang cannot affect request handling. The supervisor restarts a crashed worker with a 5-second to 5-minute backoff, stops with the web server, and does not restart a worker that stood down on an operator switch. It stays off when `SNEEK_WORKERS_DISABLED=true` (the Compose `web` service, which has a dedicated `worker` service), `SNEEK_EMBEDDED_WORKER=false`, `SNEEK_WORKERS_ENABLED=false`, `SNEEK_WEB_SCHEDULER_ENABLED=false`, or when `DATABASE_URL` is unset. `SNEEK_DISABLED_JOBS` / `SNEEK_ENABLED_JOBS` still scope it. pg-boss delivers each scheduled job once, so the embedded worker is safe beside a dedicated worker and across web replicas. `npm run dev` does not start it.

**Backlog after an outage (2026-11).** A restarted worker must not replay a stale backlog. A device push whose inbox notification is more than six hours old is marked `mobile-outbox:SKIPPED` instead of delivered (the inbox row is unchanged). A scheduled campaign more than 24 hours past its send time is not dispatched; it stays scheduled for a person to reschedule. Cron ticks missed while no worker ran are not replayed.

**iCal cadence (2026-11).** `ical-sync` runs hourly (`0 * * * *`, Sydney) instead of four-hourly, so a same-day booking reaches the schedule before its turnover. Per-feed 15-second deadline, conditional requests and the four-feed concurrency cap are unchanged.

`npm run workers:start` uses the CLI TypeScript configuration. Worker opt-in and master/per-job kill switches remain required. `SNEEK_ENABLED_JOBS` optionally limits registration to an allowlist, with `SNEEK_DISABLED_JOBS` taking precedence. `npm run workers:ical` selects only `ical-sync`; it does not opt the worker in or enable invoices, laundry planning, reminders, or status-changing sweeps. Compose forwards both lists and the master kill switch. Fresh shared heartbeats now advertise registered handlers; diagnostics distinguish worker liveness, iCal handler liveness, and recent AUTO/MANUAL sync history. Missing/unavailable evidence is not success. (2026-10)

Selected schedules (see `workers/boss.ts` for the complete current registry):

| Job | Cadence (Sydney) | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| `ical-sync` | every 4 h | `syncAllIcal` — enabled feeds with a URL, AUTO attribution (detail in Section B) |
| `reminder-dispatch` | every 30 min | 24h/2h job reminders (`lib/ops/reminders.ts`) |
| `cleaner-day-reminder` | every 30 min | day-of high-alert reminder to the assigned cleaner (web+email+sms per prefs); dispatcher gates on 6 AM / within-2h and de-dupes per cleaner+job per day |
| `job-task-auto-approve` | hourly | auto-approves stale pending client job-task requests |
| `case-follow-up` | every 4 h | follow-ups on stale cases |
| `stock-alerts` | every 2 h | inventory threshold alerts |
| `admin-attention-summary` | hourly | digest of items needing admin attention (also carries laundry attention) |
| `tomorrow-prep-dispatch` | every 2 h | tomorrow-prep summaries at the configured time |
| `workforce-post-dispatch` | hourly | scheduled workforce hub posts |
| `email-campaign-dispatch` (2026-07 changes) | **every 5 min** | scheduled marketing email; runs at the scheduler's floor so a 09:00 campaign goes at 09:00. Double-dispatch is impossible: each dispatcher claims its row with a conditional `updateMany` status transition (legacy `scheduled→sending`; engine `SCHEDULED→SENDING`), and the two dispatchers exclude each other's rows |
| `marketing-campaign-dispatch` | every 5 min | the newer campaign engine's dispatcher (`lib/marketing/campaign-sender.ts`) |
| `sla-escalation` | hourly | SLA warn/overdue escalation (`lib/ops/sla.ts`) |
| `safety-checkin-alerts` | every 30 min | overdue safety check-ins |
| `timing-rule-reconcile` (2026-07) | every 30 min | one-shot repair of jobs whose start/due drifted from admin timing rules before the iCal fix; gated on the `timingRuleReconcilePending` flag — runs once, clears the flag, then every tick is a cheap check (`lib/ops/timing-rule-reconcile.ts`) |
| `stale-en-route-sweep` | every 30 min | reverts jobs abandoned EN_ROUTE (no arrival in 6 h) back to ASSIGNED |
| `auto-pause-stale-jobs` (2026-07) | every 30 min | closes the clocks on jobs stuck IN_PROGRESS > 24 h and moves them to PAUSED so they land back on the admin radar (`lib/ops/auto-pause.ts`) |
| `daily-ops-briefing` | daily 07:00 | morning ops briefing |
| `daily-invoice-generation` | daily 08:00 | auto-invoices every user due per their cadence (`lib/finance/cadence.ts`, `auto-invoice.ts`) |
| `xero-invoice-sync` | hourly :20 | reads Xero status and payments for client invoices and credit notes in Xero; records new Xero payments through the payment path; no-op when Xero is not connected (`lib/billing/client-invoice-xero-sync.ts`) |
| `pending-pay-approval-reminder` | daily 09:00 | nags on pay approvals sitting pending |
| `recurring-job-generate` | daily 03:00 | materialises recurring jobs over the lookahead window (settings-gated) |
| `unfinished-job-push-reminder` (2026-07) | daily 17:00 | end-of-day push-only nudge to cleaners with genuinely stale unfinished jobs (PAUSED/IN_PROGRESS scheduled before today); de-duped per job per day (`lib/ops/unfinished-reminders.ts`) |
| `laundry-driver-nudge` (2026-07) | daily 15:00 | per-driver push catch-up on stale tasks (due today still PENDING/CONFIRMED, or PICKED_UP > 24 h) and auto-ABANDONs any ACTIVE route left from a previous day (`lib/laundry/reminders.ts`) |
| `location-pings-cleanup` | daily 03:00 | deletes `CleanerLocationPing` rows older than 7 days |
| `accountability-nightly` | daily 20:00 | quality-streak + monthly-ranking bonus proposals — creates PENDING pay adjustments only, manager approves (`lib/accountability/streaks.ts`) |
| `google-reviews-refresh` | daily 03:00 | refreshes the public-site Google reviews cache (24 h TTL — weekly left it stale 6 of 7 days) |
| `cleaner-property-stats` | daily 02:00 | rebuilds the per-cleaner-per-property duration model behind the QA portal's live "EST finish" / suggested visit order; pure cache, safe to re-run |
| `weekly-laundry-plan` | Mon 09:00 | drafts the week's laundry plan |
| `document-expiry-check` | Mon 08:00 | expiring workforce documents |
| `recognition-check` | Sun 09:00 | workforce recognition milestones |

### Operational status and delegated corrections (2026-10-09)

Estate Jobs view administration now lives under the page-heading three-dot menu → View options. The panel groups sort, density, list/board layout, columns, personal saved views and team defaults. Reset appearance preserves filtering; Reset Jobs view resets the full view. Hidden controls remain mounted so initial personal/team defaults still apply and drafts survive closing the panel. Shared publication permissions and revision checks are unchanged.

Job details use shared SVG milestone vectors (`JobStatusProgress`/`JobStatusIcon`), horizontal on desktop and vertical on phones. Estate Jobs rows/cards animate the actual status briefly; reduced motion disables animation. The Jobs filter disclosure combines search, date, status and refinements with one clear action; choosing a date shortcut replaces custom dates. Visible lists refresh every 15 seconds/on focus, retaining stale rows with feedback if a background read fails.

Job/laundry cards share whole-block status tints in `lib/jobs/status-presentation.ts`. Job detail workflow progress keeps pauses and continuation approval in the Cleaning stage. Estate admin laundry now exposes range/search/status filtering, Sydney-day totals, active evidence and handoff detail, task-scoped reports and audited replacement photos. Failed loads retain a clear error rather than reporting zero work.

`ops_feature_permissions_v1` supports optional sensitive grants on custom packs and manager assignments. Grants default off and are verified server-side alongside existing feature restrictions and the manager's own PIN/password. Supported grants cover job deletion/reset, property archive, client/case/template deletion, protected laundry deletion and Xero reconciliation. PIN setup remains in Profile. Owner-only account credentials, role administration and impersonation remain separate.

`POST /api/admin/invoices/[id]/reconcile` records a manually reconciled Xero void using an explicit acknowledgement, reason/reference and sensitive credentials. It locks the invoice, rejects payment evidence/pending export, releases existing billing allocations and audits the transition atomically. It keeps the invoice, lines and Xero identity; it performs no Xero network request. Paid invoice credit/refund workflows are not bypassed.

Dashboard totals refresh while visible every 30 seconds and on focus. Live GPS retains device measurement time; its heartbeat obtains a new fix instead of re-dating cached coordinates. The request indicator remains open during long operations and gives readable completion/error time, correctly handling HTTP failures.

### A8. GPS & Live Ops

**Cleaners were shown client/office correspondence (2026-08).** Before every clean the start briefing read out "client requested a reschedule", "client asked for an ETA", "client asked for the report", the reschedule reason and the approval. None of it is work and none of it is the cleaner's business. **Two independent sources:**

1. `POST /api/cleaner/jobs/[id]/start` built its own briefing query as `where: { jobId }` — every task on the job. Those correspondence rows ARE stored correctly with `visibleToCleaner: false`, but the briefing filtered on `source` and `approvalStatus` only, so an approved reschedule request (approved = true, visible = false) walked straight through. Fixed in **both** places: the query now filters, and `resolveStartBriefingItems` skips anything explicitly hidden or already completed regardless of what a caller hands it — the caller-side filter is what went missing once and would go missing again. Only an EXPLICIT `false` hides a row, so rows predating the field still show.
2. `applyReschedule` (`lib/phase4/analytics.ts`) appended `Reschedule reason: …` to `JobMeta.internalNoteText` — the note read out to the cleaner — and it accumulated, so rescheduling twice meant two lines forever. The reason was already on the `JOB_RESCHEDULE_ASSISTANT_APPLY` audit entry, which the admin job page renders in its **Activity** tab, so it now lives only there: visible to the people it concerns, invisible to the one it does not.

The rule the cleaner surfaces follow: **`visibleToCleaner` is the single authority**, and `listCleanerJobTasks` already honoured it — only the hand-rolled briefing query did not.

**Compliance documents were invisible in v2 (2026-08).** Nothing was lost at the database or API layer — `StaffDocument` rows were written and reachable — but three things together meant an owner could not find them. (1) `components/v2/admin/workforce/compliance-board.tsx` fetched every document and then rendered only `attention` (expired / expiring / pending), so a **current, verified** police check, white card or signed policy appeared **nowhere**: precisely the documents you open the page to confirm. (2) The v2 admin sidebar had no Workforce entry at all — the only route in was System → Workforce → Review compliance, where v1 had it one click away. (3) No per-person list existed in EITHER v1 or v2: both profile pages showed three counts and no way to open a file.

Fixed by adding an **All documents on file** section (search + category + status filters) beside the attention list, both rendering the same `ComplianceDocRow` so they cannot drift; a Workforce entry in the v2 sidebar plus the missing Coaching tab in the workforce sub-nav; and a real document list on the account profile with open links, expiry dates and status. That card now renders **even at zero** — hiding it when empty made someone with nothing on file look identical to someone fully compliant.

**Lapsing credentials (2026-08)** — `lib/workforce/credential-expiry.ts`. `User.vehicleRegoExpiry` and `User.driverLicenseExpiry` have existed for a long time and **nothing had ever read them**: no job, no reminder, no screen, so a licence could lapse in silence. `User.visaExpiry` is new (migration `20260822000000_add_user_visa_expiry`) and joins the same sweep rather than getting one of its own, because three dates with three sweeps is how two end up wrong. Warning windows differ by what renewal actually costs: **60 days for a visa** (an application, a decision and a wait — a fortnight's notice is not actionable), 30 for a licence or rego. `runCredentialExpiryCheck` sends admins **one digest**, not one push per person, and notifies the admin side only — a cleaner knows when their own visa expires; what was missing was the business finding out before rostering them onto work they are no longer permitted to do. Days are counted in whole **Sydney** days so a credential expiring today reads as 0 all day instead of flipping negative at some hour.

**Cleaners are not told about proposals they never made (2026-08).** The system proposes bonuses on its own — a streak of ten cleans at 97+, a monthly ranking — and when an admin declined one, the cleaner was emailed "Pay addition declined". They had not asked for it and did not know it existed, so the first they heard of a $40 bonus was being told they were not getting it. `shouldNotifyPayee` (`lib/finance/pay-adjustment-notify.ts`) draws the line on **who asked**: `CleanerPayAdjustment.source` non-null means the system proposed it, null means a person did. Gated inside `notifyPayAdjustmentOutcome` rather than at its four call sites, because a rule split across call sites is how one of them ends up wrong.

**It bends in exactly one place, deliberately:** anything that MOVES money is still sent. An approved deduction (`REWORK_DEDUCTION`, `RECTIFICATION_DEDUCTION`) is money leaving someone's pay, and taking it silently because "they did not ask for it" would be indefensible — the reason to suppress the decline is that *nothing happened*, not that the cleaner is uninvolved. So only `REJECTED` on a system-proposed row is suppressed; approvals, amount changes and reversals all still go. The separate Phase-8b bonus channel in `app/api/admin/pay-adjustments/[id]/route.ts` was doing the same thing a second time (it is the one that named the streak in the email) and now fires on APPROVED only.

#### Barcode inventory — quick scan and printed labels (2026-08)

**The mode is sticky, and that is the whole ergonomic idea.** Pick Add / Remove / Set / Check / Move once, then scan repeatedly. Asking "what should this one do?" after every read doubles the taps and nobody does it twice. Because it is sticky it must also be impossible to misread: the selected mode is large, coloured, and repeated over the camera, and the two destructive modes carry the danger colour — the failure that matters is a cleaner in Remove mode who believes they are in Add mode, quietly emptying a shelf.

**Quick scan is NOT a count run.** `count-run.ts` reconciles the whole cupboard and zeroes anything unscanned; quick scan touches only what you point at. Mixing them would mean topping up one shelf silently emptied the room. Both exist because they answer different questions.

**The mode lives in a ref as well as state.** The scanner's `onScan` callback is created once, so a plain closure would capture whichever mode was selected when the camera started and apply it for the rest of the session — silently, and only for people who changed mode mid-scan.

**Undo works by SET, not by inverse.** Reversing an Add with a Remove is wrong the moment anything else touched that shelf in between; setting the recorded previous value puts it exactly where it was.

**Printed labels** (`lib/inventory/label-codes.ts`, `label-sheet.tsx`): the barcode is rendered as **SVG, never canvas** — a canvas barcode prints at screen resolution and the bars blur together on paper, which is the commonest reason a printed barcode will not scan. Every label carries human-readable text including the code itself, because a barcode nobody can read without a scanner is useless the moment the scanner fails. `break-inside: avoid` on each label stops one being split across a page break, and the print stylesheet hides the app chrome so a shelf tag does not come out with a sidebar beside it. Two layouts: many-per-page for setting up a property, one-per-page for a replacement.

**Generation skips items that already have a label for that scope**, and says so. Re-minting would orphan every tag already stuck to a shelf: the printed one stops resolving and nobody finds out until a cleaner scans it and gets nothing.

**Discoverability:** labels and the consolidated shopping list are separate ROUTES, not `?tab=` views, so the inventory chip bar cannot reach them — they are linked from the inventory page header instead. Left out, they would have been invisible in exactly the way the compliance board was.

#### Barcode inventory — stage 3: shopping (2026-08)

**A duplicate was written and deleted before it shipped.** A `lib/inventory/shopping-needs.ts` was built to derive what needs buying — and `getShoppingListRows` (`lib/inventory/shopping-list-report.ts:25`) already did exactly that, with the same trigger (`onHand <= reorderThreshold`) and the same quantity (`parLevel - onHand`). Two copies of that arithmetic is precisely the failure mode this codebase keeps hitting, so the new file was removed and the work rebuilt as `consolidateShoppingListRows`, an ADAPTER over the existing rows. The printed sheet, the emailed list and the shopping screen now cannot disagree about what to buy.

**The shopping list is DERIVED, never stored.** That is why a finished count changes what the shopper sees immediately — there is no second list to regenerate, go stale, or forget. `GET /api/inventory/shopping-needs` returns all three shapes at once (per-property needs, per-item totals, per-supplier baskets) because they cost the same rows and making a client re-fetch to switch views would feel slow for nothing.

**Three views, three questions, and where you are standing decides which:** BY ITEM in the aisle ("how many bottles do I pick up?"), BY SUPPLIER planning the trip ("what can I get in one shop?"), BY PROPERTY at the car ("what comes out of the boot here?"). By item leads because it is the only one that answers the question you have while the list is actually open. Every item row still carries its per-property split — six bottles with no idea where four of them go is a number you cannot act on once you have paid. The by-property view is derived client-side from the same rows rather than fetched, so it cannot disagree with the item view.

**`notifyScanCountCompleted`** closes the loop the owner asked for: a finished count pushes to the office instead of waiting to be found. It renders through the SAME `buildShoppingListHtml` as the manual email — the version nobody proofreads is the one that goes out automatically — and is **silent when nothing is below threshold**, because mailing "nothing needed" after every count is how a useful alert becomes something people filter away. Fired after the transaction and never awaited into the response: the stock is already written, and a mail server having a bad afternoon must not tell a cleaner their count failed.

**Bug found in passing:** `getShoppingListRows` had no `item.isActive` filter, so a discontinued product below threshold appeared on every shopping list forever. A list with permanent residents is one people stop reading to the bottom of. Now excluded — which fixes the printed sheet and the emailed list too.

#### Barcode inventory — stage 2: the scanner (2026-08)

**Why ZXing rather than the browser's own decoder.** `BarcodeDetector` is built into Chrome on Android and hands decoding to the OS — fast, and good in poor light. Safari does not implement it, and because Apple requires every iOS browser to use WebKit, **no iPhone has it, including Chrome for iOS**. `@zxing/browser` decodes in JavaScript and works everywhere, more slowly. `barcode-scanner.tsx` uses the native decoder when it exists and falls back silently, because a cleaner does not care which one ran. The owner chose this over an Android-only build or waiting for native Expo scanning; the native path stays open, and adopting it later touches only this component.

**The per-code cooldown is what makes it usable.** A decode loop pointed at a label fires every frame, thirty times a second.
