One pre-existing trap found on the way: `preferred-cleaner/route.ts` holds TWO portal contexts (`portal0` is the real one); the audit initially pointed at the wrong one and tsc caught it. **No migration.**

- **2026-08-24** — **Job hours could be set but never cleared — plus a correction to what the recurring-hours fix claimed.** `estimatedHours` was `z.number().positive().optional()`, so a blank box sent `undefined`, and the PATCH route builds its Prisma payload with `const data = { ...body }` — an absent key is not an instruction to clear, it is no instruction at all, so the old value survived every attempt to remove it. Now `.nullable().optional()`, matching `fixedPrice` two lines below, which had used exactly that convention for a clearable field the whole time. Both form call sites send `null` instead of `undefined`; the CREATE route coerces back to `undefined`, where null and undefined both honestly mean "not set". Zero is still rejected: a 0 is an empty box everywhere in this codebase and null is how you say "empty" here, so accepting both would give one meaning two spellings. **THE CORRECTION:** the entry above for `lib/ops/recurring.ts` said a job created with no hours "was worth nothing to whoever cleaned it". That overstates it. `computeCleanerPay` deliberately falls back to the cleaner's CLOCKED timer hours when no allocated time is set — documented as "never fabricate hours" — so such a job pays the time actually worked, and pays nothing only if the cleaner also never clocked in. The recurring fix was still right (allocated time is the intended basis, and clocked time can differ from it), but the severity as written was wrong. That same fallback is what makes CLEARING hours a legitimate mode rather than data loss: it is how an admin says "pay them for the time they actually spent". `tests/lib/job-hours-clearable.test.ts` (7 tests) is also **the first test of any kind over `lib/validations/job.ts`**, which is a large part of why the gap survived. **No migration** — `Job.estimatedHours` was always nullable in the database; only the validator disagreed.

- **2026-08-24** — **Multi-role was half-shipped: the CLEANING rail still asked `where: { role: CLEANER }`.** `58aa6973` made PERMISSION checks role-aware (`requireRole` passes if any HELD role satisfies) and widened the MAINTENANCE candidate queries. It did not touch the **data queries** on the cleaning side, and since `User.role` is now only somebody's PRIMARY hat, every one of those was asking "whose main job is cleaning" while meaning "who can clean". Two silent failure shapes. **GATES:** `app/api/admin/jobs/[id]/assign` looked the user up with a scalar role match and rejected anyone it could not find — telling somebody who holds CLEANER as a second role *"Only active CLEANER users can be assigned"*. Same in bulk-assign, dispatch auto-assign apply, the cleaner-to-cleaner TRANSFER target lookup, the client's preferred-cleaner picker, and the QA rework payee picker. `app/api/jobs/[id]/form` was worse still: it gated the transfer control on `session.user.role === Role.CLEANER`, so a multi-role cleaner never saw the option at all. **SWEEPS:** payroll, dispatch candidates, the recurring generator's active-cleaner set, day reminders, tomorrow-prep, accountability streaks/coaching/overview, availability, client portal cleaner lists and job-task notification recipients all built their working set from a scalar match, so a multi-role person was simply ABSENT with nothing raised. **Payroll was the expensive one — no row, no pay.** Its base query is widened AND `extraPayeeIds` now derives from JOB ASSIGNMENTS as well as adjustments and QA inspections, which is deliberately role-agnostic: the question payroll should ask is "who did the work", and an assignment is the record of that. That also picks up somebody DEACTIVATED after working, who previously vanished from the run still owed for days they had done. `lib/booking/requests.ts` was not cosmetic either — its cleaner count is booking CAPACITY, so an uncounted multi-role cleaner understated it and turned work away. New `lib/auth/role-query.ts` (pure, 8 tests) holds the one expression, matching what `lib/maintenance/assignments.ts` already did inline. **DELIBERATELY NOT CHANGED:** the ~25 `session.user.role === Role.CLEANER` checks that RESTRICT scope ("if you are a cleaner, only show your own jobs"). Those are the "WHERE AM I" question, not "MAY I", and widening them to held roles would wrongly restrict an ADMIN who also holds CLEANER. They should move to the ACTIVE role, which is a separate security-adjacent change. **No migration.**

- **2026-08-24** — **A SEVENTH assign path: cleaner-to-cleaner transfer wrote no `payRate` at all.** The previous correction fixed the paths that resolved the rate *wrongly*; `grep "payRate: settings.cleanerJobHourlyRates"` cannot see a path that never writes `payRate` in the first place. There are **ten** `jobAssignment.create/upsert` sites. Six now share `resolveAssignmentPayRate`. Four write no rate — and three of those are **correct**: rework pay is governed ENTIRELY by `reworkPayAmount` (null → $0), never hours×rate, and that is enforced identically in `lib/finance/job-pay-for-cleaner.ts`, `lib/finance/summary.ts` and `lib/finance/payroll.ts`, so a `payRate` on a rework assignment is dead data. The fourth was real: `app/api/cleaner/jobs/[id]/assignment-response/route.ts` TRANSFER creates an assignment for a different cleaner on **ordinary** work, where hours×rate genuinely is the mechanism — `computeCleanerPay` fell through to the live per-cleaner rate and never saw `Property.cleanerServiceRate`. The rate is now stamped on BOTH upsert branches, because reactivating a previously-removed assignment is a fresh offer (PENDING, `respondedAt` cleared) and should not inherit whatever was stamped the last time that person was on the job. **The lesson, third time around: grep for the WRITE SITE (`jobAssignment.create`/`upsert`), not for the wrong expression.** A field that is silently absent looks identical to a field that is correct. Still 0 of 19 properties carry a `cleanerServiceRate`, so nothing changes today. **No migration.**

- **2026-08-24** — **Correction: the hours normalisation reached two of five readers, and the two it missed disagreed with each other.** The commit before it converted `lib/ical/sync.ts` and `lib/jobs/window.ts` and stopped there. Three more sites resolved clean hours privately, and the two QA endpoints **contradicted one another on live data**: `app/api/qa/jobs/[id]/progress/route.ts` put the property's `assignedCleaningHours` AHEAD of the job's own `estimatedHours`, while `app/api/qa/jobs/[id]/route.ts` put the job first — so the same job reported different expected hours depending on which endpoint the inspection workspace happened to call, and expected-versus-actual is the number a clean gets scored against. The detail route also read only those two fields, so a property carrying only `cleaningDurationMinutes` or the legacy onboarding value reported NO expected hours at all. Both now defer to `resolveJobCleanHours`: the job's own hours are a decision about that job and win, then assigned → minutes/60 → legacy. The progress route keeps its declared **degraded-database contract** — the optional columns are still fetched in their own try/catch so a missing migration degrades one field rather than the endpoint; `accessInfo` simply joins that same query. Third site: `lib/ops/recurring.ts` copied `rule.estimatedHours` straight onto the job, and a recurring rule is allowed to carry none — `Job.estimatedHours` is the ONLY input to `computeCleanerPay`, so such a job was worth nothing to whoever cleaned it, with no error anywhere. It now falls back to the property's hours (the rule still wins when it has a value), reading them from the property query the generator already makes. **This is the second time in two days a "one shared rule now" commit turned out to have converted only some of the call sites** — the rate one had the same gap. `grep` for the field, not for the function, before believing a rule is shared. **No migration.**

- **2026-08-24** — **Correction: `cleanerServiceRate` reached only HALF the paths that assign work.** The commit that wired up `Property.cleanerServiceRate` claimed "all THREE dispatch paths share one pure rule". That was wrong, and the wrongness was the same shape as the bug being fixed. There are **six** places that stamp `JobAssignment.payRate`, not three — the three named (admin assign, bulk assign, preferred-cleaner on job creation) plus **ops auto-dispatch** (`lib/ops/dispatch.ts`), **recurring job generation** (`lib/ops/recurring.ts`) and **continuation handover** (`lib/jobs/continuation-requests.ts`). Those last three each kept their own `settings.cleanerJobHourlyRates?.[id]?.[type] ?? undefined` and never consulted the property. The live effect would have been a property paying its configured rate when assigned by hand and silently not when the same work arrived from a recurring rule, ops dispatch, or a handover — the exact inconsistency the original change existed to eliminate, left running in half the codebase. All six now share `resolveAssignmentPayRate`. The continuation path keeps its existing-assignment rate ahead of the property's, since a rate already agreed for THAT job is a decision about that job; the property rate is the floor beneath it. `lib/ops/recurring.ts` gets the rate from the property query it already makes, so no extra round-trip. Still 0 of 19 properties carry a `cleanerServiceRate`, so nobody's pay changes today — which is also why nobody had hit the inconsistency. `grep cleanerJobHourlyRates` is how to check a seventh path has not appeared. **No migration.**

- **2026-08-24** — **One answer for "how long does a clean take here".** Three fields hold it — `assignedCleaningHours` (hours), `cleaningDurationMinutes` (minutes), `accessInfo.defaultCleanDurationHours` (legacy hours) — the property API writes all three independently from three separate form inputs, and nothing reconciles them. **The damage was never the drift itself; it was that different code read different fields.** That is precisely how a property edited to 4 hours on its own page kept producing 3-hour jobs from iCal. A count against the database first: all 19 properties carry `assignedCleaningHours`, all 19 carry the legacy value, **0 carry `cleaningDurationMinutes`** (which is nonetheless read in 45 places), and **all 19 agree** — so this is a latent hazard rather than live corruption, and normalising the readers changes nobody's hours today. `lib/properties/clean-hours.ts` (pure, 17 tests) is now the single precedence: assigned → minutes/60 → legacy, with a zero at any level treated as an empty box rather than "this clean takes no time", since no form offers a way to mean that and a job with no allocated hours pays nothing. `lib/ical/sync.ts` and `lib/jobs/window.ts` now defer to it instead of carrying private copies. **`resolveExpectedHours` in `lib/jobs/window.ts` had a second, quieter instance of the same bug**: it never consulted the legacy field at all, so a property holding only that value reported NO expected hours. It is now corrected — but the honest note is that **it has no production callers**, only tests, so nothing was actually going wrong through it. That is a finding of the same family rather than a live fault: an exported helper that nothing reads. Its doc comment was also stale, still describing a precedence the code had already stopped using. One test changed with it: `estimatedHours: 0` used to return 0 through a `??`, and now falls through like every other empty box — `lib/validations/job.ts` rejects a non-positive estimate so no form can send one, and 0 of 400 jobs carry a zero. Both new-job forms were already correct. Also exported: `cleanHoursSource` (which field answered) and `cleanHoursDisagree` (do they actually conflict) — with every reader now agreeing, drift is harmless, but a property whose page says 4 while its onboarding value says 3 is still one somebody will eventually argue about. **No migration.**

- **2026-08-24** — **`Property.cleanerServiceRate` was write-only dead config, and now works.** Editable on the property form, validated, saved by the API, and described at line 362 of this very document as a "per-clean override of hourly maths" — and **read by nothing**. An owner setting it saw no effect and no error: the same shape as the three-hour pay bug. A count against the database first showed **0 of 19 properties had a value**, which is what made this safe to fix rather than a live-money gamble — nobody's pay changed on the day it shipped. ("Nobody used it" is confounded evidence, incidentally: it did not work, so anyone who tried saw nothing happen and stopped.) **It is captured at DISPATCH, not read at pay time.** `computeCleanerPay` receives a job, not a property, and all eight of its callers use narrow `select`s — threading a live property lookup through them would have meant eight queries each having to remember one column, and the first that forgot would make an invoice disagree with a payroll run about the same job. `JobAssignment.payRate` is already the dispatch-time snapshot every one of those eight reads, so the rate goes in there. Precedence: per-cleaner job-type rate → property rate → the person's own default → global (which flags `rateMissing`). **A stored 0 counts as unset on both sides** — neither form offers a way to mean a deliberate zero, and honouring one would pay somebody nothing for turning up. All THREE dispatch paths share one pure rule in `lib/finance/assignment-rate.ts` (7 tests): admin assign, bulk assign, and the automatic preferred-cleaner path on job creation. Writing it inline three times — which is what the first attempt did — would have meant the same property paying one rate when assigned by hand and another when iCal created the job. **No migration.**

- **2026-08-24** — **QA transport allowance, paid once per day worked.** Completing the owner's answer *"admin sets QA pay rate + transport allowance per day"*. **The RATE half already existed** — `qaPay.defaultMode/defaultFixedAmount/defaultHourlyRate/defaultHoursPerInspection` in settings, read by `computeQaAssignmentPay`, editable in the Safeguards settings section. Only the allowance was missing. **PER DAY IS THE WHOLE POINT:** an inspector visiting four properties on a Tuesday made ONE journey, and paying per inspection would be paying for journeys nobody made. **The day is a `Australia/Sydney` calendar day**, not a UTC one — an inspection finishing at 8pm Tuesday Sydney and one at 11am Wednesday are 09:00 and 01:00 UTC, so a naive UTC date either files them together or splits one evening across two days; either way the count is wrong, and the count IS the money. **CLAIMED, NOT DERIVED.** New `QaDayAllowance` table with `@@unique([inspectorId, day])`: deriving the allowance from whichever inspections sit on the invoice being built breaks the moment a day is SPLIT across two invoices — the payee excludes one inspection, sends the rest, then bills the last one next fortnight, and Tuesday's travel is paid twice. The row is created inside the send transaction (`createMany` + `skipDuplicates`, so a race becomes "already claimed" rather than a failed send) and stamped with the invoice id, which is what lets a VOID hand it back — a voided invoice that kept its claim would leave the day permanently unpayable, since the unique constraint means nothing else could ever claim it either. `amount` is snapshotted at claim time so a later rate change cannot re-price an invoice somebody already holds. The allowance gets its OWN section on the PDF, one line per day, each naming the day: a charge visible only in the total is a charge the payee cannot check. Rules are pure in `lib/finance/qa-transport.ts` (16 tests, including the Sydney-midnight straddle and the split-day exclusion). **NEEDS A MIGRATION:** `20260824000000_qa_day_allowance` (a new table only, additive). *Correction to an earlier note: this log previously implied the QA default rate was unbuilt. It was not — only the allowance was.*

- **2026-08-23** — **MULTI-ROLE USERS.** One person, more than one job — a cleaner who also inspects, a laundry driver who also cleans. Until now that meant two logins or a role describing half of what somebody does. New `UserRole` join table; **`User.role` stays the PRIMARY** and does not move, because it decides which portal somebody lands in, every historical row was written against it, and turning it into an array would have meant rewriting **848 authorisation call sites** in one change. An account with no extra roles behaves byte-for-byte as it did before — a test asserts that equivalence across all eight roles. **THE CENTRAL SPLIT:** *"May I?"* is authorisation, answered against every role a person HOLDS — somebody who genuinely is a QA inspector is one whichever hat they have on, and refusing them until they flick a switch adds no safety (it is one click) while breaking every cross-portal link. *"Where am I?"* is presentation, answered against the ACTIVE role — which portal, which nav, where home goes. `requireRole` takes the first; middleware's portal gates take the first; landing and nav take the second. **The active-role cookie is deliberately UNSIGNED and never trusted as a grant**: it names a role, and `resolveActiveRole` re-checks it against what the database says on every request, falling back to the primary when it does not match — which is also the revocation path, immediate rather than at next sign-in. The contrast with `sneek.test-as` is the point: that ticket IS authority (it names a user to become) so it is a signed JWE; this one names a role its holder must independently be proven to have, so signing it would protect nothing and imply a trust the code does not extend. `validate-session` and `requireSession` resolve **identically** — disagreement between them means middleware routes to one portal while the page there refuses, a redirect loop and a total outage. Impersonation RESETS the held set to the target's only; merging would leave an admin's own reach live behind a "view as" banner. Granting is ADMIN-only (not OPS_MANAGER — it is a permission change), refuses ADMIN/OPS_MANAGER as secondary hats (privilege escalation), refuses the client-scoped roles, and writes its audit row INSIDE the transaction: a permission change that cannot be traced should not stand. **Three multi-role breaks fixed with it:** `payeeRole` was copied from `User.role` and frozen into the invoice snapshot at send time, so a mixed cleaner/QA invoice would have pushed to Xero with **every line labelled as one kind of work** — lines are now tagged with their source at send time and the kind is derived from the invoice's actual contents (`lib/invoicing/payee-kind.ts`, 12 tests); the maintenance candidate queries matched primary role only and would have rejected somebody whose second job qualified them, while telling them the opposite; and the nav badge counted every assignment while its link pointed at one role's section. **NEEDS A MIGRATION:** `20260823150000_user_extra_roles` (a new table only, additive). **Known remaining:** `lib/finance/payroll.ts:29` still scans `where: { role: CLEANER }` — partly compensated by `extraPayees`, which derives from real assignment ids rather than role, and that is the pattern to copy.

- **2026-08-23** — **The self-review failsafe was guarding the wrong table.** The owner's rule is explicit: *"add a fail safe to not to assign the cleans they do and do the QA themselves. never allows this."* `mayAssignQa` implemented it for `MaintenanceItemAssignment` — a person given the QA hat on a maintenance ITEM. The rail that actually inspects and SCORES a clean is `QaAssignment`, and it had no guard on **any** of its six write paths. **Not exploitable today**, and worth stating plainly rather than dressing up: job assignment requires `role: Role.CLEANER` (`app/api/admin/jobs/[id]/assign/route.ts:79`) and QA pickup requires QA_INSPECTOR/OPS_MANAGER/ADMIN, which are disjoint sets, so no account can currently sit on both sides. The guard is being built **before** multi-role for exactly that reason — the moment one person can hold CLEANER and QA_INSPECTOR, whoever cleaned a property could claim its inspection, pass it, and invoice for the inspection on top of the clean. New `lib/qa/self-review.ts` (pure predicate + a thin DB assert, 11 tests) is called from all six: admin assign, admin **reassign** (guarding only the first assignment leaves the rule one edit from being bypassed), and the four self-claim paths — `pickup`, `timer`, `checkin` and `self-rework`. **The self-claim paths are the ones that matter**: `pickup` creates an assignment out of nothing, so guarding only the admin routes would leave the rule bypassable by walking up and claiming an inspection instead of waiting to be given one. `self-rework` is guarded because that is where the chain ends in MONEY — its `loadContext` accepts an assignment with `assignedToId: null`, so a caller need never have claimed the inspection to reach it, and could otherwise flag their own work and claim time and pay for putting it right. It THROWS rather than silently dropping the name: a quietly-refused claim leaves an inspector believing they have the job and an admin believing it is covered, and nobody inspects it. Self-review does not fail loudly — the scores come out fine, and every quality figure downstream is then derived from an inspection nobody independent ever did. **No migration.**

- **2026-08-23** — **Nobody was told their money moved.** Recording a payment against a client invoice sent no email at all, so a client who paid had no confirmation it had been applied and their next statement was the first chance to discover it had not. Marking a payee invoice PAID sent nothing either: somebody did the work, invoiced for it, and money arrived in their account with no idea which invoice it settled or whether it was the full amount. The second is the one people actually chase — a cleaner paid $940 with no breakdown cannot tell whether their fuel claim was included, so they ask, and answering that by hand every fortnight costs more than the email. Two different documents, deliberately: a **RECEIPT** to a client ("we have received this from you"), which always states the remaining balance because a part payment that reads like a full one is how an invoice stops being chased by both sides at once; and a **REMITTANCE ADVICE** to a payee ("we have sent this to you"), naming the period, the method, the account and what the payment covers — "we paid you $940" answers the wrong question. Wording is pure in `lib/finance/payment-notices.ts` (13 tests, including that the two never read as each other). Both sends are best-effort and AFTER the write: the money is already recorded, and failing the response over a mail error would tell an admin the payment did not save when it did. The remittance reads `paidAmount` rather than `totalAmount`, because those differ whenever the office settled a different figure from the one invoiced. **Also:** `recordPayment` now accepts a `receiptKey`, stored on the individual ledger entry rather than the invoice — an invoice can be paid in instalments and each one has its own proof. **No migration.**

- **2026-08-23** — **The DEFAULT invoicing cadence generated nothing, ever.** `User.invoicingCadence` is `@default(ON_COMPLETION)` in the schema and the profile UI labels that option "(default)" — and `isInvoiceDueToday` returned **false** for it, on the strength of a comment claiming invoices were "generated job-by-job, not by schedule". No such job-by-job generator has ever existed anywhere in the codebase. `listUsersDueForInvoicing` also filtered the cadence out of its query, so those clients were excluded before the rule that would have skipped them ever ran — two guards saying the same wrong thing, which is why neither looked suspicious. **Every client never explicitly moved onto a weekly, fortnightly or monthly cycle has never been auto-invoiced at all.** Not late: never. No error, no empty invoice, nothing in a log — just completed work that was never billed, discoverable only by somebody noticing revenue that should have arrived and did not. ON_COMPLETION is now due daily, with a 20-hour grace so two scheduler ticks in one day cannot produce two invoices, and `generateClientInvoice` gained `completedOnly` so that cadence bills **finished** work only — `BILLABLE_JOB_STATUSES` deliberately includes in-progress jobs for an admin generating a period invoice by hand, but a client who chose "bill me as the work is done" should not receive an invoice for a clean still underway. One invoice per day of finished work, not one per job: eleven properties should not mean eleven separate invoices. **CUSTOM still returns false, and that is correct** — no custom schedule is stored anywhere on the user, so it means the office raises those by hand; it is now a stated decision rather than an omission beside a wrong one. The existing test asserting ON_COMPLETION was correctly skipped has been rewritten. **No migration.**

- **2026-08-23** — **Send a payee's invoice back for a change.** An admin reviewing a submitted cleaner or QA invoice had exactly two options: mark it paid, or void it. There was nothing between "this is right" and "this is finished with", so one wrong line meant voiding the whole invoice and hoping the payee worked out what to alter. `CHANGES_REQUESTED` is that middle state. **The note is required** — the zod refine rejects the request without one — because an invoice returned with no explanation reliably comes back unchanged, and the payee cannot guess which of twenty lines the office disagreed with. A send-back RELEASES the work exactly as a void now does, since somebody told to fix an invoice cannot rebuild it while every line on it is still stamped as billed; it clears the stale payment record; and it emails the payee with the reason and a link into their own portal (the QA rail self-invoices here too, so the link resolves per role rather than always pointing at the cleaner portal). The stored note is cleared as soon as the invoice moves on, so a resubmitted or paid invoice does not keep showing a request that was already actioned. **The status rules are now in one place.** `lib/cleaner/invoice-status.ts` (pure, 10 tests) holds `releasesPayeeWork`, `clearsPaymentRecord`, `requiresChangesNote` and `clearsChangesNote`; the admin route had been spelling the same predicate out inline at three separate points, which is precisely how a fourth status gets added and two of the three get updated. One test asserts the invariant that binds them: nothing may release the work without also clearing the payment record, or a released invoice would show the payee as settled for work they are about to re-bill. **NEEDS A MIGRATION:** `20260823140000_cleaner_invoice_changes_requested` (two nullable columns, additive).

- **2026-08-23** — **The same void bug on the payee rail, and worse.** Earlier today the client side was fixed: voiding an invoice was stranding its shopping and maintenance items. The cleaner/QA rail had the identical defect and nobody had looked. Voiding or deleting a `CleanerInvoiceSubmission` released only the JOBS (`cleanerPaidAt` cleared); the payee's approved pay adjustments, their QA inspection fees and their out-of-pocket shopping stayed stamped with `includedInCleanerInvoiceId` — against a submission that would never be paid, or in the delete case no longer existed at all — and `getCleanerInvoiceData` excludes on exactly that stamp. Those amounts could never be billed again. **This is the worse direction of the bug**: a client under-billed is money the business can chase later, but a cleaner whose approved $80 fuel claim silently stops appearing on their invoice has no way to discover why, and the business does not find out either — the figure simply gets smaller. `releaseCleanerInvoiceConsumables` now frees all three tables inside the same transaction as the void, and inside the delete's transaction too (deleting is the more dangerous of the two: the row is gone, so nothing remains to trace the stranded stamps back to). The shopping RUN's own `cleanerReimbursementStatus: "INVOICED"` compat-JSON flag is deliberately NOT reversed here — it is display state, the settlement stamp is the actual billing guard, and editing that blob from outside the compat writer that set it is how a JSON record ends up in a shape its own parser rejects. 7 tests. **No migration.**

- **2026-08-23** — **A card payment said PAID and behaved as unpaid.** The Stripe webhook set `status: PAID` and `paidAt` and nothing else — no `paidAmount`. `outstandingOf()` derives from `paidAmount`, so every invoice ever settled by card still read as owing its full value in the finance UI and anywhere else that figure is shown. It now also writes `paidAmount` (the invoice's own total, since this settles the whole balance), `paidDate`, `paymentMethod: "STRIPE"` and the payment-intent id as `paymentReference`. **And the `ClientPayment` orphan is closed.** `/api/client/invoices/[id]/pay` created an attempt row as PENDING and **nothing in the codebase ever read or updated it** — the webhook updated `ClientInvoice` directly — so every card payment ever taken left a permanently-pending record, a payments table disagreeing with its own invoices, and nothing to reconcile a later refund or dispute against. The webhook now settles it to `SUCCEEDED`, best-effort and last, because failing there would have Stripe retry a payment that has already been applied. **Also:** a VOID invoice could still be exported or pushed to Xero. `xero-push` CREATES a real invoice in the accounting system, so voiding one here and pushing it there put a live receivable in the books for money nobody owed — recoverable only by a manual credit note, after which the books and this system disagree and the books win. Both routes now refuse with a 409. *(Correction to an earlier note in this log: those two routes were described as bypassing the status transition graph. They do not write `status` at all — the real hazard was the void export, which is what is fixed here.)* **No migration.**

- **2026-08-23** — **REVERSE, the other half of the correction pair.** The owner specified two ways to undo an invoice and only one existed. **VOID** (shipped earlier today) means *this invoice is finished with — issue a new one*, and its work goes back in the pot unpaid, which is why voiding releases the shopping and maintenance stamps. **REVERSE** means *the invoice is fine, its content is wrong*: it returns to DRAFT keeping its number, its lines and its payment history, and releases nothing, because this invoice is still the one that will bill those items. Until now the only route back from PAID was the ADMIN-only `forceStatus` escape hatch, which does clear the paid stamps but records itself as somebody dragging a settled invoice backwards by hand — indistinguishable in the audit log from a mistake. `reverse` is its own field on the invoice PATCH for exactly that reason, and writes a `CLIENT_INVOICE_REVERSED` row naming the previous status and stating `releasedItems: false` so the trail can never be confused with a void. Allowed from APPROVED / SENT / PART_PAID / PAID. **Refused from VOID** — a void has already released its items, and pulling it back to DRAFT would let it re-bill work another invoice may have picked up since, putting the same charge on two live invoices. Refused from DRAFT, which is already editable. `metadata.payments[]` is deliberately KEPT: the money genuinely arrived, what is being undone is the invoice's content, and an admin reconciling a bank statement afterwards needs that ledger. The button sits beside Void in the finance list with a title spelling out which is which, so the choice is readable at the moment of clicking rather than discovered afterwards. 16 tests on the transition module. **No migration.**

- **2026-08-23** — **Voiding an invoice no longer destroys the work on it.** The owner's rule is that a void means "resubmit a new invoice, the items stay unpaid" — and the double-bill stamps were never cleared, so a voided invoice took its shopping reimbursements and its client-paid repairs down with it. They stayed marked as billed against an invoice nobody would ever pay, no future run would pick them up, and the charge simply disappeared. Silently: the only person who could ever notice was whoever eventually wondered why a repair had never reached a bill. `releaseInvoiceConsumables` now clears `ShoppingSettlement.includedInClientInvoiceId` and both `MaintenanceItemAssignment.includedInClientInvoiceId`/`At`, inside the SAME transaction as the status change — releasing against an invoice that then failed to void would make the same work billable on two live invoices at once. **Jobs need no release**: their guard is a live scan of `ClientInvoiceLine.jobId` across non-VOID invoices, so voiding frees them by construction, which is why this was never noticed. 6 tests. **Also:** the job detail's invoice lines now show the date an invoice was PAID (`paidDate`, falling back to `paidAt`) beside its number — "PAID" with no date leaves the obvious next question to a second screen, which is where an unreconciled payment hides — and the status badge is toned, since every status rendered neutral before and a PAID invoice looked identical to a VOID one at a glance. **No migration.**

- **2026-08-23** — **Invoices are grouped by property, with extras separated.** A client with eleven properties received one flat list of forty lines in generation order, so working out what a single property cost meant reading every row and adding it up by hand — the job the invoice was supposed to have already done. `ClientInvoiceLine.propertyId` has existed as the admin's grouping hint the whole time and **the PDF never read it**: `getClientInvoice` did not even fetch the relation, so the grouping an admin chose was invisible on the one document that mattered. It is fetched now, and `lib/billing/invoice-grouping.ts` (pure, 15 tests) decides the layout. Property sections come first, alphabetically, so consecutive invoices read the same way and this month's figure can be compared with last month's without hunting; each carries its own subtotal, rounded once at the end so the sections visibly add up to the invoice total rather than trailing float noise. **Extras — shopping reimbursements and the new maintenance charges — get their own section, always last**, because burying a $400 repair inside a property's list of cleans is how a client finds it by accident three weeks later. A line's property comes from its JOB first (that is where the work happened); `line.property` is only the manual hint, used when no job sits behind the line. A line with neither still appears, under "Other charges" — a charge that vanishes from an invoice is far worse than one filed oddly. A single-property invoice with no extras gets no headings at all, since naming a section after the only property on the invoice is chrome without information. The property is dropped from each row when the section heading already names it. **Also:** the admin cleaner-invoice workspace had a status filter and no search, so a payee ringing about "PAY-0041" left the admin scrolling — the entire point of sequential numbering is lookup. Search by number or name added, and the empty state no longer says "Nothing here yet" over a search that simply missed, which read as "no invoices exist" and sent people off to ask why an invoice never arrived. **No migration.**

- **2026-08-23** — **Invoices get real numbers, and client-paid repairs finally reach an invoice.** THREE separate problems, one wave. **(1) Numbering.** A client invoice was named `INV-<yyyyMMdd>-<6 random hex>` from `randomUUID()` against a `@unique` column with no retry, and a cleaner's invoice had no number at all — its PDF derived a reference from the date range, so **every payee billing the same fortnight printed an identical "invoice number"** and accounts could not tell four documents apart. Random is also not lookupable: an accountant asked for "invoice 41" cannot find it, and cannot show an auditor that nothing was removed from the middle, because a gap between two random strings says nothing. New `InvoiceSequence` table, one row per rail (`CLIENT` → `INV-0001`, `CLEANER` → `PAY-0001`), allocated by a single atomic `UPDATE … SET "lastNumber" = "lastNumber" + 1 … RETURNING` — a table rather than a settings field precisely because a read-modify-write against JSON would hand two simultaneous invoices the same number. **The owner sets where it starts**, since this business issued invoices before this software existed. The allocator NEVER fails an invoice: if the table is unreachable it logs loudly and returns the old random format, because losing the sequence is a numbering problem that can be corrected afterwards and failing to invoice is a cash-flow problem that cannot. Rules are pure in `lib/billing/invoice-numbering.ts` (29 tests — one of which caught that `Number(null)` is `0`, so a blank field would have silently reset the counter and re-issued every number from 1). **(2) Client-paid maintenance.** `MaintenanceItemAssignment.payPayer` has had a `CLIENT` value since it was added, with a schema comment promising the amount "also lands on the client's invoice", and **nothing ever implemented it** — an admin could agree a $400 repair, record CLIENT, complete it, pay the worker, and never bill the client. `generateClientInvoice` now includes completed CLIENT-paid assignments, priced through the same `resolveAssignmentPay` the worker's own screen uses so the client is billed the figure the worker was shown, with no markup. `includedInClientInvoiceId`/`At` is the double-bill guard, stamped in the same transaction as the invoice for the same reason the shopping stamp is. Rules pure in `lib/billing/maintenance-billing.ts` (12 tests). **(3) Two status-integrity bugs.** `POST /api/admin/invoices/[id]/send` set `SENT` unconditionally, ignoring the transition graph entirely — sending a VOID invoice put a cancelled document back into the client's outstanding balance, and sending a PAID one left the payment stamps on a row the rest of the system then read as unpaid. Both are now refused with a 409; re-sending a PART_PAID invoice is still allowed (chasing is routine) but no longer downgrades its status. And `getFinanceHubSummary` counted only `SENT` as outstanding, so the moment a client paid **any** part of an invoice the whole balance left receivables — a $4,000 invoice with $200 against it removed $4,000 from the figure and left $3,800 owed that the KPI strip said nothing about. It now sums `total − paid` across SENT **and** PART_PAID, clamped at zero per invoice so an overpayment on one cannot mask a debt on another. **NEEDS TWO MIGRATIONS:** `20260823120000_invoice_sequence`, `20260823130000_maintenance_client_billing`. ⚠️ **The owner must set the starting invoice number in Settings before the first invoice goes out**, or numbering begins at 1 and may duplicate numbers already sent from the previous system.

- **2026-08-23** — **ELEVEN SCHEDULED JOBS HAD NEVER RUN.** There are two schedulers: the pg-boss worker (`workers/boss.ts`, preferred) and a fallback inside the web process (`lib/ops/web-scheduler.ts`) for a single container with no worker. The fallback stands itself down as soon as `isDedicatedWorkerActive()` sees a pg-boss schedule — which is precisely what the worker creates. So every job registered ONLY in the fallback has never fired on a real deployment. Eleven were: `credential-expiry-check` (the visa / driver-licence / vehicle-rego warning the owner asked for), `missed-clock-in-sweep` (the forgotten-clock-in failsafe, also owner-requested), `stale-en-route-sweep`, `auto-pause-stale-jobs`, `cleaner-day-reminder`, `pending-pay-approval-reminder`, `unfinished-job-push-reminder`, `laundry-driver-nudge`, `qa-auto-score` (so a submitted clean nobody inspected sat in QA_REVIEW forever, holding up the cleaner's pay), `timing-rule-reconcile` and `cleaner-property-stats`. All eleven are now registered on the worker at the Sydney hour the fallback pinned them to, each behind the existing `jobEnabled()` guard so `SNEEK_DISABLED_JOBS` still works. The failure mode is what makes this worth recording: the code existed, was unit-tested, and was documented as shipped — the only symptom was an absence, a reminder nobody received and a sweep nobody noticed not happening. `tests/lib/scheduler-parity.test.ts` now reads both files as text and fails if any fallback job is missing from the worker, so a job added to one side alone cannot ship again. **No migration.**
- **2026-08-23** — Admin can file a compliance document on someone's behalf again (`components/v2/admin/workforce/compliance-board.tsx`). v1 had this and v2 did not: the board could review a document and request one, but not upload one, so a certificate handed over on paper or by email could only be filed by asking the staff member to upload something they had already given you. The endpoint (`UPLOAD_DOCUMENT` on `POST /api/admin/workforce`) was never the missing piece — it has always accepted this payload; the screen was. The file is uploaded BEFORE the row is created, because a document row whose link goes nowhere reads in a compliance list as a lost certificate rather than a failed upload. An optional link to an outstanding request closes that request instead of leaving it on the chase list beside the document that answered it, and the picker is scoped to the chosen person so an admin cannot close somebody else's request.

- **2026-08-23** — Cleaner dashboard order: Today's brief now sits ABOVE My day. The brief is the orientation — weather, what the day looks like, anything the office wants said before work starts — and it had been rendering below the job timeline and the Needs Attention feed, which is late enough that a cleaner has already decided how to run the day before reading it. Owner-requested.
- **2026-08-23** — **Maintenance assignment: the half that was never reachable.** CP-6 shipped the multi-role roster panel, its three endpoints and its emails — and `MaintenanceRoleAssignmentsPanel` was **imported into `maintenance-detail.tsx` and never rendered**, so from the office the entire feature looked unbuilt: there was no way to put a cleaner or a QA inspector on a maintenance item. It is now mounted at the top of the Assignment column. Three more inputs that had no output are closed with it. **(1) Pay.** `payType/payAmount/payHours/payPayer` were rendered on the assignee's phone and written by nothing, so every assignment read "no pay set" — and the assignee could already press "this price is wrong", which wrote a `PENDING` request that no screen in the system could approve or reject. New `PATCH /api/admin/maintenance/assignments/[id]` (`SET_PAY | APPROVE_PAY_CHANGE | REJECT_PAY_CHANGE`) is the missing half; it is a **separate route** from `/api/maintenance/assignments/[id]`, which is the assignee acting on their own row and is scoped to `session.user.id`. Pay is validated through `resolveAssignmentPay` — the same resolver the portal and the invoice read — so an admin cannot save an hourly rate with no hours and have it render as "no pay set" on a phone. The payee is emailed only when the figure actually moves. **(2) Lifecycle.** `acceptedAt/declinedAt/declineReason/completedAt/completionNote` were being written by the assignee and shown to nobody; the admin's only readout was "assigned", which does not separate *on their way* from *has not opened the email*. `ASSIGNEE_SELECT` now carries them and each roster row states its status, with a decline shown loudly and with its reason. **(3) Instructions.** `assignmentInstructions` was read by `assigned-maintenance-section.tsx` and written by nothing, so the column was permanently null and the portal's instructions card never once appeared; `PATCH /api/maintenance/[id]` now accepts it (admin/ops only — a client editing them would be briefing a worker outside anything the office priced) and round-trips it through `parseInstructions`, so what is stored is exactly what the assignee sees. The editor offers TEXT/PICKUP/CONTACT and deliberately **not** PHOTOS: the reader understands it, but there is no admin upload path yet, and a kind you can pick and cannot fill reads as broken rather than unbuilt. **THE QA FAILSAFE IS NOW ENFORCED.** `mayAssignQa` had existed and been unit-tested since CP-6 and **nothing called it** — the rule lived in the test suite and nowhere a save could reach. `setItemAssignees` now asks it before every write, in **both directions**, because a one-way check is defeated by the order of the saves (assign QA first, then cleaner, and a QA-only guard waves it through). For QA the disqualifying set also includes the cleaners on the JOB the item came from. It throws rather than silently dropping the name. **No migration.**
- **2026-08-23** — **Scan tasks get a front end; the cleaner supplies page gets its properties back.** `ScanTask` + `/api/scan-tasks` shipped the day before with no UI at all, so nobody could create one and nobody could see one outstanding. Admin: new `scan-tasks` tab and KPI tile on `/v2/admin/inventory`, with an "ask someone to count" modal. The POST's `notified` flag is reported to the asker **verbatim** — a false there means the email never left and the assignee has no idea they were asked, and saying "they've been notified" in that case is the one lie that guarantees the count never happens. Cleaner: an open-task count joins the dashboard's Needs Attention feed and a list of the actual asks sits **above the tabs** on `/v2/cleaner/supplies` — a request filed behind a tab is a request nobody sees. The assignment email now deep-links `?task=<id>`, the stock-count screen shows who asked and why, and `startedAt` is stamped on open (nothing else in the app wrote it). Completion is stamped **server-side inside the apply transaction** via `updateMany`, whose WHERE clause is the authorisation (task must belong to this caller AND this property) — done client-side, a tab closed between two calls would leave someone permanently owing a count they had already done. Both new counts are failure-tolerant (`safeCount` / `.catch`) because `ScanTask` is unmigrated on live and a missing table must cost one tile, not a cleaner's whole morning dashboard. **Also fixed in passing:** `/v2/cleaner/supplies` fetched its property list only when the *shopping* tab was active, but the **default** Quick scan tab renders `<QuickScanLauncher properties={properties}/>` — so opening Supplies always showed an empty list with nowhere to scan.
- **2026-08-23** — **The three-hour pay bug.** `Job.estimatedHours` is the only value cleaner pay is computed from, and for every iCal-created turnover it was built from `cleaningDurationMinutes`, else the legacy `accessInfo.defaultCleanDurationHours` (which the property CREATE form defaults to `"3"`). It **never** read `assignedCleaningHours` — the field the property page edits, and the one the schema itself calls the first-class source for expected clean length. Hence the reported loop: edit the hours, it saves correctly, and tomorrow's sync rebuilds the jobs at 3h. Nothing was overwriting the edit; the edit was never being read. `lib/ical/sync.ts` now prefers it. Two further places ignored the same field: the v1 new-job form prefilled from the legacy value only, and the v1 property page had **no `assignedCleaningHours` input at all** — on that skin there was genuinely no way to set the number that drives pay. **Still open from the same investigation:** `Property.cleanerServiceRate` is write-only dead config, editable in both UIs and read by nothing, despite line 362 of this document claiming it overrides hourly maths; three overlapping hours fields (`assignedCleaningHours`, `cleaningDurationMinutes`, `accessInfo.defaultCleanDurationHours`) drift with nothing syncing them; and `lib/validations/job.ts` uses `z.number().positive()`, so job hours can never be cleared or set to zero from the UI.

- **2026-08-05** — **v2 cutover (Phase 0.1).** Estate is now the default look everywhere a person ENTERS the app: the house default (`DEFAULT_SETTINGS.defaultPortalVersion`), the store parse (only an explicit `"v1"` opts out) and `effectivePortalVersion`'s final fallback all resolve to v2, and unauthenticated `/login` follows the look to `/v2/login` with `callbackUrl` preserved — previously a signed-out person met the classic login before being routed into Estate. v1 stays fully routable: deep links are never rewritten, and `?look=v1` still switches login *and* portal roots back, verified in both directions live. Also confirmed by tree-wide grep that no v2 component links or navigates into a v1 route.
- **2026-08-04** — Commercialisation plan added at `docs/COMMERCIALISATION_PLAN.md`: six phases from foundation hardening (v2 cutover, self-serve onboarding, per-tenant module flags) through multi-tenant activation, a pilot program, PMS integrations (Hostaway → Guesty → Hospitable), paid launch (Starter/Pro/Scale AUD pricing), moat features (AI QA pre-scoring, payments margin, guest trust page, native wrappers) to expansion bets. Documentation only — no code changes; awaiting owner approval to begin Phase 0.
- **2026-08-04** — Upload watchdog removed: the temporary diagnostic (client component + unauthenticated debug sink) shipped during the upload hunt was deleted after the owner confirmed the fix below on the phone; its lessons are summarised in A6.
- **2026-08-04** — Cleaner photo upload: the ACTUAL root cause. The B1 pause-revalidation listener made the job workspace re-run its full `load()` on every `visibilitychange`/`focus`, and `load()` opened with `setLoading(true)` — swapping the workspace to the loading skeleton and **unmounting the file input**. On a phone the picker always hides the tab, so returning with a photo fired this instantly: the change event had no input left to land on. Seen as "quick flash, nothing uploads"; desktop file dialogs don't hide the tab, so it never reproduced locally; laundry has no such listener. Fixed: only the first load may blank the screen — refreshes update in place, the form stays mounted, the pick lands. Verified by simulating the hide→return→pick cycle against the real report form (input survived, upload 200, photo counted). The 2026-08-03 full-resolution-decode fix below remains a real memory fix, but this listener was the deterministic trigger.
- **2026-08-03** — Cleaner photo upload root cause fixed: gallery picks (the main cleaning-report path) were still decoded at full resolution by `browser-image-compression` — 190–430 MB of RGBA for a modern phone photo, which Android answers by killing the tab, seen as "quick flash, nothing uploaded". Cleaner-only because only evidence flows compress client-side; laundry (no image processing) worked on the same phone. `compressImage` now routes through the same `createImageBitmap`-scaled decode as camera stamping (`downscaleImage`), capping peak memory at the 2400 px working bitmap for every capture surface. See A6.
- **2026-08-03** — Upload watchdog (temporary diagnostic): after two plausible fixes failed to close the "picking a photo refreshes the page and nothing uploads" report, and a real 3000x2250 JPEG driven through the real v2 capture component in a real browser produced zero teardown events locally, the app now instruments itself in production instead of being reasoned about — in-flight upload counting, a wrapped `location.reload` that captures the caller's stack, and two independent teardown detectors (`sendBeacon` on `pagehide`, plus a `sessionStorage` breadcrumb that survives the reload and reads the navigation type) reporting into the server log. Both files are marked for deletion with the bug. See A6.
- **2026-08-02** — Email controls, events and reassignment (Batch A wave 4, part 2): the audience x kind matrix and per-person preferences became editable in the comms control center (the settings zod had to declare `audienceKinds` or the grid saved into nothing); three silent changes gained notifications — extra tasks added to an assigned job (which notified nobody at all), and job-note edits, whose detector tested the payload rather than diffing, so an unchanged re-save announced a change and CLEARING a note announced nothing; and replacing a job's primary cleaner stopped auto-sending "meet your cleaner" and now returns an editable `CLEANER_REASSIGNED` draft that a person confirms, with the edited text being what actually sends.

- **2026-08-02** — Email gating, narrowest-last (Batch A wave 4, part 1): the only controls were global, so silencing one person's reminders meant silencing every cleaner's. Added a per-audience override of each kind and per-person preferences (`UserEmailPreference` + `User.allEmailOff`), evaluated per recipient so a multi-recipient send drops only those who opted out; every link fails open, and `critical` auth mail bypasses all of it. Backfilled `kind:` onto thirteen automatic sends that were unswitchable — which is why the `pay_adjustment` and `workforce_update` toggles existed in the UI while controlling nothing — leaving admin-clicked sends deliberately ungated. Migration: `20260803000000_email_preferences`. Still to come in this wave: the admin matrix/per-user UI, the reassignment confirm modal, and the three new cleaner event kinds.

- **2026-08-02** — Money features (Batch A wave 5): pay adjustments gain a category and a derived `taxable` flag, so parking and receipts print in their own non-taxable invoice section instead of inflating what the payee is recorded as having earned; the header drops "Tax" because no GST is applied. Payees can claim an invoice paid (`PAID_CLAIMED`) and admins confirm it from a new Approval Center queue — the claim never writes the payment record itself. Adjustments can be removed from an invoice (`excludedAdjustmentIds`, filtered before the partition so per-job totals and settlement stamps stay consistent, and the row stays owed). Fixed: the admin invoice status PATCH cleared the entire payment record on every non-PAID status, wiping the audit trail for money that had actually left. Migration: `20260802020000_pay_category_and_invoice_pay_claim`.

- **2026-07-29** — Forms wave: deterministic template resolution + job-pinned quote forms, builder impact panel, 25-field-type end-to-end audit (Yes/No boolean fix, condition operators, section logic, real preview), duplicate-as-draft fix + starter blueprints; client services agenda + nav promotion.

- **2026-08-13 (b)** — Client portal, wave 2 (filters). **Jobs board**: the board persists the last-used filter to `localStorage` and silently restores it, so a client who once picked "Today" saw only today's jobs on every later visit with nothing on screen explaining the short list — an active-filter bar now reads "Showing N of M jobs" with per-chip removal and a Clear all. Added admin-parity filters: property, job type, and an explicit inclusive from/to date range. **Reports**: added a real from/to date range (plain GET form, so the page stays a server component and works without JS) plus an "All time" period — the widest previous option was the current calendar year, making anything before 1 January unreachable; a custom range overrides the period chips rather than silently contradicting them. Rows now carry weekday, suburb and an "Open job" link.
- **2026-08-13** — Client portal, wave 1. **Laundry page fix (P0)**: the page defaulted to a *week* window anchored on today, so a client with no laundry in the current week saw two empty cards and read the page as broken — an "All" filter mode was added and is now the default, and the page's `.catch(() => [])` now logs instead of silently turning a query failure into an empty page. Laundry confirmations collapsed behind a "N updates · show details" expander (the "Latest update" box already carries the headline). **Nav restructure**: `NavItem` gained an optional `group?: string` and `PortalShell` renders a small heading when the group changes (other five portals omit it and render unchanged); the client nav now lists every destination under Overview · Your cleans · Your homes · Support · Billing · Account, with no "More" catch-all — the mobile bottom bar's first five are Home, Jobs, Laundry, Approvals, Reports. `/v2/client/services` was the jobs list under a second name (same `listClientJobsForUser` query) and is now a redirect to `/v2/client/jobs`.
- **2026-08-12 (d)** — Settings page regrouped: the 23-chip flat strip became a labelled grouped rail (Overview · Company & brand · Notifications · Money · Cleaners & quality · Operations · Access & permissions · System) — all notification surfaces sit together, all money together, etc. Tab keys and `?tab=` deep links unchanged.
- **2026-08-12 (c)** — Admin quick QA scoring: v2 regains v1's "close a submitted job with a score", but suggestion-first. `lib/qa/suggested-score.ts` reads the cleaner's submission (unanswered required fields, unticked checklist items, missing photos, no-photo waivers, incomplete job/admin tasks, self-inspection gaps) and proposes a score with an itemised reason per deduction; `GET /api/admin/qa/awaiting` lists every SUBMITTED/QA_REVIEW job with **no** real inspection plus its suggestion, and `POST /api/admin/qa/bulk-quick-score` applies them in bulk (Quality page → "Awaiting QA — quick score", pre-selected and pre-filled so the default action is one click). `lib/qa/auto-score.ts` shares one applier between the admin path (kind `ADMIN`) and the hourly `qa-auto-score` sweep (kind `AUTO`), which closes out anything still uninspected after `settings.qaAutomation.autoScoreAfterHours` (default 24h, 1–720, editable in Settings → Operational safeguards alongside a master `autoScoreEnabled` toggle). **A real inspection is never overwritten** — any job carrying a `kind:"QA"` review is excluded from the queue and refused by the applier (`REAL_QA_EXISTS`), and job status always derives from `lib/qa/authority.ts`.
- **2026-08-12 (b)** — Admin nav attention badges: red per-item counts on the v2 admin rail + mobile tabs (`lib/admin/attention-counts.ts` → `GET /api/admin/nav-attention`, polled 60s/on-focus by the admin layout). Approvals mirrors the fifteen all-approvals queues exactly; Jobs = upcoming unassigned; Quality = unclaimed inspections; QA Issues = pending rectifications; Cases = OPEN/TRIAGE; Lost & found = REPORTED; Laundry = FLAGGED; Finance = payee invoices awaiting (SUBMITTED/PAID_CLAIMED); Growth = NEW leads. Pages with no unambiguous pending queue deliberately carry no badge.
- **2026-08-12** — NOIMG: "no photo taken" option on upload fields for admin-exempted cleaners (coded reason required, overrides the photo requirement, amber display in the report, automatic QA points deduction per waived field; Settings → Accountability roster).
- **2026-08-11 (b)** — Laundry truth: report renders from the LaundryTask (not stale form answers); a late final submission can no longer silently undo a confirmed-and-notified pickup (admins get a contradiction alert instead). Admin GPS edits now recompute `gpsDistanceMeters` against the property pin so the report's distance figure follows the corrected coordinates.
- **2026-08-11** — Report clock truth (TimeLog-derived clock-in/out on the Estate report; submission time never presented as clock-out) + admin clock/GPS editing in the v2 job Activity tab (durationM recompute, audit-logged, cleaner notified, report auto-refresh; edit forms stack below the table so phones aren't overflowed). Report presentation: uncropped click-to-open photos, larger white-letter cover logo via `logoDarkBgUrl`, GPS attendance card. Public report verification: per-job crypto-random codes + QR on the report, anonymous `/verify` + `/verify/[code]` pages with a dedicated minimal public view model and per-IP rate limiting (migration `20260811000000_report_verification`).
- **2026-07-29** — Estate report template (new default; faithful per-field rendering via a tested view model, page-break-aware PDF), stage-relevant date chips across the laundry boards, and the Estate loader for all v2 portals.

- **2026-07-29** — Pay transparency & edit-anywhere: canonical per-job pay summary (payee-grouped, invoice-equal), shared adjustment display/edit component with automatic-vs-manual origin, edit-or-navigate on every pay surface, correcting-adjustment flow for settled money.
- **2026-07-27** — Migration-drift cleanup (stray `template_engine_v2` record removed from dev DB; m7-templates branch untouched). This document created.
- **2026-07-27** — Shopping settlement stamps: reimbursements + shopping time get independent payroll/invoice stamps with frozen amounts and a sentinel backfill for historically invoiced runs (closed the fourth and last double-pay stream).
- **2026-07-27** — Phase-4 pay runs: stamp all pay streams they pay (not just jobs), widen payees to inspectors/adjustment-only cleaners, signed line amounts.
- **2026-08-01** — QA ownership, scoping & pause (B1): canonical `lib/qa/ownership.ts` (visibility vs payee); self-picked-up inspections now appear on QA pay and are payable on both settlement rails (they were permanently unpayable); reviews page scoped to the viewer (it had no `where` clause at all) and the queue's completed scope no longer drops ownership; `qaAutomation.assignedOnly` setting replaces role-inferred scoping; "Continue review" for in-progress inspections; cleaner Pause/Resume promoted to the always-visible job header; workspace revalidates so a server-side geofence pause stops the ticking clock.
- **2026-08-01** — QA annotations + PDF alpha (B2): shared `lib/qa/annotation-composite.ts`; markup flattened onto its photo at submit time (verdict, section, damage and rework scopes) and self-healed on read for existing issues, so cleaners finally see what QA circled; admin workspaces stop rendering the bare overlay; the QA report renders one opaque image instead of CSS-stacking layers; `pdf.ts` keeps alpha images as PNG — fixing the black-background signature and the black tiles that hid every annotated photo; cleaner feed shows the admin-configured category label.
- **2026-08-01** — QA report rebuilt (B3): Estate skin with `@page`/`break-inside` rules and a version stamp; now queries `qaIssues` + the cleaner's submission media (it previously queried neither, which is why an accountability-graded inspection had no pictures at all); presigns every image key instead of emitting relative `publicUrl` paths that resolved to nothing in the PDF; inlines the signature as a data URL so it is never flattened onto black; adds the score breakdown, per-item findings, rework flagged areas and an attendance/GPS block; new `cleanerSafe` mode (costs, rework pay and private notes stripped in the data) pinned by session role, gated by `QAReview.cleanerReportVisible` with an admin toggle, and downloadable by cleaners from their QA feedback card. Migration: `20260801000000_qa_report_cleaner_visibility`.
- **2026-08-01** — QA result email (B4): replaced a two-line stub that named no actual issue with a full Estate-branded message — settings-driven score arithmetic, every finding with category label, description, guest-ready flag and flattened markup photo, a "what to focus on next" derived from recurring categories, and a substantive plain-text fallback; fires on legacy-template inspections (previously silent) and from the admin QA save path (previously silent); notifies every assigned cleaner, not just the primary; CTA repointed to `/v2/cleaner/quality`.
- **2026-08-01** — QA form (B5): admin accountability settings finally reach the inspection form (they never had — every configured category and deduction value was invisible to inspectors); issue taxonomy expanded 14 → ~50, add-only; new `lib/qa/generate-from-checklist.ts` derives a per-property QA template from the property's own cleaner checklist, mirroring section titles verbatim and collapsing each section to a handful of Pass/Minor/Fail outcomes plus a Common-mistakes section; `isSystemManaged` replaces the name-prefix auto-upgrade that silently clobbered admin-edited templates; `sourceFormTemplateId` + drift detection; `QaAssignment.draft` for server-side autosave; the "How was it done?" verdict block now uses an allowlist so it stops grading the inspector's own notes. Migration: `20260801010000_qa_template_provenance_and_draft`.
- **2026-08-01** — On-site check-in gate (B6): distance was computed at every check-in and never once acted on — a start 40 km away returned `200 OK` with a friendly distance, and a geolocation permission denial produced an info toast and clocked in regardless. Now GPS is hard-required to clock in (blocking dialog with per-platform re-enable steps), a fix beyond 150 m returns `409 OFF_SITE_REASON_REQUIRED` and records nothing until the cleaner picks one of 8 coded reasons (admin push + `GPS_CHECKIN_OFF_SITE` audit + shown in admin job details), the same gate applies to QA check-in, coarse fixes (>200 m accuracy) are judged neither way rather than accusing someone standing in the kitchen, `Property.geofenceRadiusM` allows a per-property radius, and an on-site check-in stamps `Job.arrivedAt` — which previously only an inside-fence ping could set, meaning auto clock-out could never fire for a cleaner whose phone never produced one. Migration: `20260801020000_on_site_check_in_gate`. Deferred: the server-side departure sweep, the stale-ping fallback and the tracker's job-attribution fix.
- **2026-08-01** — Admin job detail tabs (B7): the Manage modal is retired. New `ETabs` primitive (link-based, so the page stays a server component) and `lib/jobs/detail-tabs.ts` turn the 942-line flat card stack into eleven `?tab=`-deep-linkable sections, each editable inline via `JobManagePanel` — the modal's own sections moved across verbatim, same endpoints and payloads. Three concerns that had two disagreeing homes were resolved rather than reproduced: laundry (page-read, modal-write under *Billing*) became its own section beside the laundry task, the pay ledger moved from People to Money, and continuation requests + skip joined Schedule. The board's Manage button now opens the job at `?tab=schedule`.
- **2026-08-02** — QA assignment from the calendar agenda: `GET /api/jobs` now carries each job's active QA assignment, and every agenda row offers a QA button beside Assign/Reassign, opening the same `QaAssignPanel` the job detail page uses. Dispatch and inspection scheduling happen in one sitting; making the planner open each job to assign an inspector is what left inspections unassigned.
- **2026-08-15** — Reschedule destroyed the job's metadata (audit find). `Job.internalNotes` is **not free text** — it carries the whole serialised `JobMeta` blob. `applyReschedule` (`lib/phase4/analytics.ts`) appended `"Reschedule reason: …"` to it as a plain string, making the JSON invalid; `parseJobInternalNotes` then fell back to defaults and the *next* writer re-serialised those defaults as clean JSON, permanently laundering the loss. Destroyed on every approved reschedule: timing rules (`earlyCheckin`/`lateCheckout`), `cleanerPayouts`, `transportAllowances`, `specialRequestTasks`, `attachments`, `additionals`, `serviceContext`. Corruption was **unconditional** on the client-reschedule approval path (`lib/job-tasks/service.ts` passes a hardcoded non-empty reason). Invisible because the `startTime`/`dueTime` columns were already stamped, so the job still looked right — only the cleaner's briefing gate vanished, and payouts reverted to a plausible hours×rate weeks later. The reason now goes into the blob's own `internalNoteText`, matching the QA route's existing "preserving structured meta" pattern. Pinned by `tests/lib/job-meta-roundtrip.test.ts`, which also asserts the old concatenation loses everything. **This was the only place in the codebase concatenating into a JSON-bearing column.**
- **2026-08-15** — `EN_ROUTE` never reached four status lists (audit find). The enum member is real and written by `app/api/cleaner/jobs/[id]/start-driving/route.ts`, but four copies of `ACTIVE_JOB_STATUSES` were never updated. Worst: **`lib/ops/sla.ts`** excluded en-route jobs from the overdue query entirely, so a job whose cleaner had tapped "start driving" was never warned or escalated however far past `dueTime` it ran — precisely the state a running-late job sits in. Also `lib/client/portal-data.ts` (job vanished from the client's board mid-drive, reappearing at `IN_PROGRESS`), `app/v2/client/page.tsx` (home card skipped today's job and advertised tomorrow's), and `lib/dashboard/immediate-attention.ts` (under-counted "ongoing today"). All four now include it. Ten copies of this list exist across the tree with four distinct definitions — worth consolidating into two named exports ("job is live" vs "cleaner currently holds work") before the next status is added.
- **2026-08-15** — The two permanently-red tests are fixed; the suite is green (1,660 passing). Both had been carried as "known-bad, ignore", which meant any *new* breakage in those two files was invisible and every shard run had to be eyeballed against a remembered baseline. **`tests/lib/qa-report-modes.test.ts`** was never flaky in the usual sense: it did `await import("@/lib/reports/qa-report")` *inside* the test body, charging the whole report module graph (template, PDF and image helpers) to whichever test ran first against the 5s test timeout. In isolation there was headroom; on a loaded shard there was not — hence "fails only under `--shard=2/3`", and hence a *second* test in the file starting to fail once the shard grew by ~90 tests. `vi.mock` is hoisted above imports, so a **static** import keeps the mocks and moves the cost into the untimed collection phase. **`tests/components/address-autocomplete.test.tsx`** was pointing at a real gap rather than being wrong: `components/shared/address-search-input.tsx` degraded to a plain text box when Google Places could not load and said *nothing*, so a user got no dropdown and could not tell whether lookup was broken or they were mistyping. It now renders "Address lookup unavailable — type the full address manually", gated on a distinct `unavailable` state (not `!ready`, which is also the initial loading state) so it cannot flash while the SDK is still loading. ⚠️ That notice is **production-visible** in any environment without a working Maps key — intended, and consistent with the documented no-key degraded mode.
- **2026-08-15** — Per-role access guides + guided route (ACCESS-1): admin now authors access instructions **separately for cleaners and laundry**. Each access-guide entry carries an optional `audience` (`CLEANER | LAUNDRY | BOTH`); **absent means BOTH**, so every entry written before this existed keeps showing to everyone rather than silently vanishing from a portal. The **"same as cleaner" flag** lives on `accessInfo.laundrySameAsCleaner` (JSON — **no migration**, and preserved across saves by the ACCESS-4 merge): when on, laundry reads the cleaner's steps *as well as* any laundry-only ones, so a property can be "same as cleaner, plus here is where the bags go" without duplicating the guide; when off, laundry never sees cleaner-only entries. `entriesForAudience` is that rule, covered by tests including the no-leak case. New kinds `KEY_PICKUP` and `LAUNDRY_PICKUP` join the existing `PARKING`/`ENTRY`, giving the four the brief names. The **guided route** is `components/v2/shared/access-route.tsx`, built on ACCESS-2's `sequence`/`orderedRoute` rather than a second model: the numbered list renders unconditionally and the animation is decoration on top of it, because the brief requires it to "read as a numbered list too" and because that is what keeps it working with no Maps key, with motion disabled, and on a bad signal in a basement carpark — exactly where somebody is standing when they need it. `prefers-reduced-motion` is honoured by **not running the step timer at all** (a static, fully readable list) rather than by shortening it, and the media query is watched live so changing the OS setting takes effect immediately. Each pinned step links out to Google Maps as its *primary* affordance, so the component never depends on the SDK loading — the documented no-API-key fallback is preserved. `GET /api/cleaner/property-access/:propertyId` now serves **both** readers rather than gaining a duplicate laundry route: it accepts `CLEANER` and `LAUNDRY`, authorises each differently (a cleaner via a job assignment at the property, a laundry user via `propertyIsVisibleToLaundry` — the same team scoping every other laundry surface uses), and filters by audience before returning.
- **2026-08-15** — Maintenance assignment rule (CP-8, partial): the owner-confirmed rule is *"clients may assign directly to a maintenance worker OR to admin; from admin, admin reassigns to anyone, carrying the full record across and auto-creating the job task on attach."* Of that, only client→worker already worked (`assignWorkerId` on `PATCH /api/maintenance/[id]`, already open to the owning client). Two halves were missing. **(1) Client→admin.** A client who does not know who should do the work had no way to hand it back — they could only pick a named worker. `assignWorkerId: null` is now that action, deliberately the *same field* so a client who can assign can also route to admin without a second permission surface; `routeMaintenanceToAdmin` clears the worker, returns an ACKNOWLEDGED item to OPEN so it re-enters triage, and records an event. **(2) Auto-create the job task on attach.** New `attachJobId` (admin/ops only — putting work onto a scheduled job is a dispatch decision) sets `jobId` *and* creates a `JobTask` titled `Maintenance: …`, `visibleToCleaner` and `requiresPhoto`, tagged `metadata.maintenanceItemId`; without it, attaching moved a foreign key and the cleaner who turned up never learned there was maintenance to do. It fires only when the job actually *changed*, so re-saving does not mint duplicates, and the task write is best-effort so it cannot roll back the attach. **"Carrying the full record across" needed no code** — reassignment updates the same row, so title, photos, quote, cost approval, the CP-7 case link and CP-6 role assignments travel by construction; what must *not* travel is the previous worker's visit, hence `visitFieldsToClear` re-arms every stamp (a stale `arrivedAt` would show a new worker as already on site somewhere they have never been). Rules are pure in `lib/maintenance/assignment-routing.ts` (16 tests). **No migration.** ⚠️ **Scope note:** CP-8 was briefed as "the biggest, full agreed design in the task", and that design was lost before this work started. Only the assignment rule above — the one part quoted verbatim in the handoff — is implemented. The wider maintenance platform remains unbuilt and unspecified.
- **2026-08-15** — Bin room + bin chute, and one waypoint model (ACCESS-2): bins existed only as a `BIN_ROOM` access-guide entry with no level and no chute — a cleaner could see that a bin room existed without knowing which floor it was on. New `BIN_CHUTE` kind (the chute is a different place from the room) plus three optional fields on the **shared** access-guide entry: `level` (free text — "B2", "Ground", "Level 3" all occur), `locationNote`, and `lat`/`lng`. **The ACCESS-1 boundary decision is deliberate and recorded here**: the brief said bins "likely share ACCESS-1's waypoint model — decide together so they don't duplicate", so rather than invent a bins-only structure that ACCESS-1 would later duplicate, the fields went on the entry every access point already uses, and `sequence` (1-based visit order) was added alongside them. An animated route "showing where to go in sequence" is an *ordering of* these waypoints, not a parallel list — `orderedRoute()` is that ordering and deliberately omits unsequenced entries, because a made-up order is worse than none when someone is following it around a building at 7am. Photos and access notes needed nothing new: they were already `images` and `instructions`. **No migration** — `Property.accessGuide` is JSON and every new field is optional, so stored entries stay valid. The kinds list, entry schema and sanitiser were also extracted to `lib/properties/access-guide.ts` (15 tests): they had been copy-pasted into both API routes and both editors, so adding a kind previously meant editing eight files and hoping none drifted — the same duplication that caused the laundry-allocation wipe. The cleaner view shows level, location and an "Open in Maps" link that appears only when a pin was actually set, so it needs no Maps API key.
- **2026-08-14** — Properties tracking stock with no stock (CP-5): a property that tracks stock must actually HAVE stock. `applyDefaultStockToProperty` had exactly **one** caller — the create-property POST — and even there it was gated behind a non-empty item selection, so every other route that turns tracking on left `inventoryEnabled = true` with **zero `PropertyStock` rows** and an empty supplies/restock/shopping screen. The dominant cause was migration `20260802000000_enable_inventory_on_active_properties`, which blanket-flipped the flag on every active property and created nothing: on the local copy **all 19** active properties are `inventoryEnabled = true` (both create forms default it to *false*, so a uniform true is the migration's fingerprint) and **7 of 19** hold no stock rows at all. Onboarding-survey approval never seeded either — a latent second path. `ensurePropertyDefaultStock` (`lib/inventory/default-items.ts`) is now the single idempotent entry point (upsert with `update: {}`), called from the create POST, `PATCH /api/admin/properties/[id]`, and onboarding approval; an empty selection now means "the standard set", not "nothing". The PATCH path seeds on *flag true AND zero rows*, so re-saving a broken property self-heals it, and seeding is best-effort on the approval and PATCH paths (logged, never fails the save). Bulk repair for the rest: `scripts/backfill/backfill-property-default-stock.ts`, dry-run by default, `--apply` to write. Note seeding remains create-time-only in nature — properties stocked when the catalog had 14 defaults still hold those 14 and did not gain the 11 later additions.
- **2026-08-14** — Shopping-run grouping (CP-5): `groupShoppingRows` (`lib/inventory/shopping-grouping.ts`) is a pure function bundling a run's lines **by property, item or supplier**, shared by the cleaner run and the client run so both agree. Item mode merges an item across properties ("grab 6 shampoos once, split later", folding custom purchases by name since each mints a throwaway `custom:<uuid>` id); supplier mode folds on case/whitespace and buckets blanks under "Unassigned supplier". Rows keep object identity so write-back by `itemId + propertyId` still works. The **default** mode is an admin setting rather than a hardcode — Inventory hub → Shopping runs → Default grouping — stored in `AppSetting` under `inventory_shopping_grouping_v1` (`{ mode }`, **no schema change**) via `lib/inventory/shopping-settings.ts`, served by `GET/PUT /api/admin/inventory/settings`; shoppers can still switch per run. Stock-run number fields were also shrunk to `h-8 w-[4.25rem]` with `inputMode="decimal"`, and Par/Min are hidden rather than disabled for roles that cannot edit thresholds — roughly halving row height on a phone. Regression: `tests/lib/shopping-grouping.test.ts`.
