- **2026-08-14** — Client counter-offers on approvals (CP-3b): a priced extra was previously take-it-or-leave-it — the client could only approve or decline, so any "yes, but not at that price" happened over the phone and never reached the record. New `COUNTERED` status plus `counterAmount` / `counterNote` / `counterAt` / `counterByUserId` on the approval record. **No migration** — client approvals are JSON in AppSetting `client_approvals_v1`, not a Prisma table. `POST /api/client/approvals/[id]/counter` mirrors the respond route's access checks exactly (countering *is* a response: anyone who may decline may counter) and only a `PENDING` request may be countered, so a client cannot reopen a settled item or counter twice without admin coming back. The request's own `amount` is deliberately left untouched — the two figures side by side are the point — and admin's Approval Center shows both with a one-click **Accept &lt;counter amount&gt;** that sets the agreed amount as well as the status, beside **Approve original**. Moving off `COUNTERED` by any path clears the proposal (`updateClientApprovalById`), because a stale counter beside a newly agreed amount is how the two sides end up arguing about different numbers. Critically, `COUNTERED` is **not** terminal: the admin queue (`all-approvals`), the nav attention count (`lib/admin/attention-counts.ts`) and the dashboard banner (`lib/dashboard/immediate-attention.ts`) all had to widen from `status: "PENDING"` to PENDING-or-COUNTERED, or a counter-offer would have vanished the instant it was sent — the same failure mode as the client-approval history gap fixed earlier this month. The counter is also written to approval history as an `EDITED` decision.
- **2026-08-14** — Damage cases raise their own repair (CP-7): a DAMAGE case is something physically broken, but reporting one used to end at the case — somebody had to notice and raise the maintenance item by hand, and once both existed nothing kept them agreeing, so a client could see a closed damage case beside a still-open repair. `createCase` now calls `autoCreateMaintenanceForDamageCase` post-commit: DAMAGE + a linked property earns a `PropertyMaintenanceItem` (title prefixed `Damage:`, priority from case severity — CRITICAL→URGENT, HIGH→HIGH, LOW→LOW, else MEDIUM — category `OTHER` because guessing one from free text mis-files repairs, `source: CLIENT`, client-visible, case attachments carried across as `photoKeys`). New nullable `PropertyMaintenanceItem.caseId` FK is the link (`ON DELETE SET NULL` — deleting the paperwork must orphan the repair, never delete it, because the damage still exists). Sync runs **both ways**: a case status change carries to the repair (`syncMaintenanceFromCase`, from `updateCase`) and a repair status change carries back to the case (`syncCaseFromMaintenance`, from `updateMaintenanceStatus`, deliberately *after* its transaction so a sync failure cannot roll back a recorded status change). The mapping is deliberately partial in both directions — WAITING_CLIENT says nothing about whether the plumber has been, and a repair merely ACKNOWLEDGED or ORDERED should not reword the client's case — and `isMeaningfulChange` is the loop guard that stops A→B→A bouncing forever. Notifications: client gets email + SMS, admin/ops get email, and admin SMS is reserved for CRITICAL only (a text per damage report trains everyone to ignore them). Every write and send here is best-effort and logged — a lost automation is an inconvenience, a lost damage report is a client dispute. Rules are pure in `lib/cases/damage-maintenance.ts` (20 tests); orchestration in `lib/cases/damage-maintenance-sync.ts`. Migration: `20260814030000_damage_case_maintenance_link` (additive, nullable).
- **2026-08-14** — Maintenance multi-role assignment (CP-6): new `MaintenanceItemAssignment` table + `MaintenanceAssigneeRole` enum (`MAINTENANCE | CLEANER | QA`). One row is one PERSON in one ROLE, so a trade, a cleaner and a QA inspector can all be on the same item and stay **separately addressable** in the admin UI instead of collapsing into one mixed assignee list. Follows the `JobAssignment` convention — rows are never deleted, they are stamped `removedAt` (null = active), so un-assigning keeps the history. `notifiedAt` records when the "you've been assigned" email actually went out, so a re-offer can email again without spamming; only newly-`added` assignees are emailed (`diffAssignees`), so re-saving an unchanged roster notifies nobody. Email is best-effort — wrapped per recipient and logged, never able to fail the assignment. Each role's portal (`/v2/cleaner/maintenance`, `/v2/qa/maintenance`, maintenance) shows the section only while that user holds an active assignment: the nav asks `GET /api/maintenance/assigned/summary` (session-scoped count), and the page itself re-checks `requireRole` and scopes its query to `session.user.id` — so the gate is not nav-only and the route cannot be walked by URL. Legacy single `PropertyMaintenanceItem.assignedWorkerId` is untouched and still drives the ad-hoc trade's visit lifecycle. Pure rules in `lib/maintenance/assignment-roles.ts`, covered by `tests/lib/maintenance-assignment-roles.test.ts` (18 tests, including that a removed assignment stops granting access even though its row survives). Migration: `20260814020000_maintenance_multi_role_assignment` (additive: new table + enum only).
- **2026-08-14** — Job details showed the WRONG guest (CP-9 follow-up): the Guests (iCal) card read `Job.reservation`, which is the booking whose *checkout* triggered the turnover — i.e. the guest who just left. The arriving guest is a different reservation, already captured on the job as `jobMeta.reservationContext`. New `lib/jobs/guest-summary.ts` (15 tests) derives an admin view and a cleaner view; admin leads with **Arriving next** and demotes the departing booking into a collapsed block; cleaners get a **Next guest** block with name, country (from the feed's free-text `locationText`), arrival, headcount and a tap-to-call button, via the additive `nextGuest` field on `GET /api/jobs/[id]/form`. `buildCleanerGuestSummary` withholds the guest's email, profile URL and reservation code from cleaners, asserted by test.
- **2026-08-02** — Pay decisions the cleaner can read: new `CleanerPayAdjustment.decisionMessage`. Wave 1 stopped `adminNote` leaking to cleaners but left a rejected request with no explanation at all — worse than the leak. Investigating that turned up two things: the admin edit box was labelled "Admin note (visible to the payee)" while writing the private field, and the decision push/email was sending that private note straight to `cleaner.email`. The explanation now has its own field, the box writes it, a separate box holds the private note, and the notification carries the cleaner-facing text. Migration: `20260802010000_pay_adjustment_decision_message`.
- **2026-08-02** — Cleaner supplies scope, nav badges, mobile jobs board (Batch A wave 3): one `active AND assigned` predicate (`lib/inventory/cleaner-scope.ts`) replaces four disagreeing scopes — the shopping-plan route and the cleaner branch of `stock-runs.ts` had **no assignment filter at all** and listed every property in the business, and restock was missing `isActive`. Data migration `20260802000000_enable_inventory_on_active_properties` turns the flag on for active properties (kept as the opt-out). New `GET /api/cleaner/attention-counts` finally supplies the `NavItem.badge` the shell has always supported — pending offers, unacknowledged QA fails, pending pay requests, immediate-attention items — rendered as red 9+-capped pills (gold is the decorative brand accent, not an alert); a failed count silently renders no pill rather than breaking the nav. Cleaner bottom tabs become Today/Jobs/Schedule/Pay/More with Route and Supplies moving to the rail. Admin jobs board: row actions are visible on touch instead of hover-only (they were invisible on a phone), and the bulk bar floats clear of the tab bar and scrolls sideways rather than wrapping into a block that covered the rows it acts on.
- **2026-08-02** — Cleaner timing visibility + start briefing (Batch A wave 2): the early-checkin/late-checkout derivation moved to `lib/jobs/timing-badges.ts` and the pills are back on the cleaner's own list and Today screens — v1 showed them, the v2 rebuild kept only the in-job banners, so a cleaner planning tomorrow saw no hint that a job cannot be started when they intended. New start briefing gates the FIRST clock-in on having read the job's timing rules, admin/approved-client tasks and note (`409 START_BRIEFING_REQUIRED`, acks content-hashed into the job meta so an edited instruction re-arms the gate). Correction to the plan: the `startVerification` payload it called dead is still read in ~10 places by the v1 cleaner page, so it stays.
- **2026-08-01** — Money correctness & pay privacy (Batch A wave 1): two leaks of admin-private data to cleaners closed — `describeAdjustment` now takes a REQUIRED audience so the payee's own job screen no longer receives `Admin: {adminNote}`, and `GET/POST /api/cleaner/pay-adjustments` answers through an explicit DTO instead of a whole-row spread that shipped `adminNote`, `reviewedById`, `sourceKey` and both settlement stamps (settlement is now a derived boolean). D1: the cleaner invoice's hours-override branch re-read the raw job meta and discarded the rework override, so an hours adjustment on a rework job reverted it to hourly pay and an unpaid rework started billing — it now uses `effectiveCustomPayout`. R9: fixed pay is labelled as such wherever pay renders (invoice prints "Fixed" with no hours, pay card reads "Fixed pay (flat amount)", the admin input is renamed from "Custom payout"). D4: `showHours` reaches the invoice preview route, which previously ignored it while download and send honoured it — the preview disagreed with the PDF the payee had just approved.
- **2026-07-26/27** — QA inspector self-invoicing: shared invoice access gate, profile banking for inspectors, QA-portal invoices screen, role-aware labels/exports; expected-invoices forecast includes inspectors.
- **2026-07-26** — QA inspection pay wired into the cleaner-invoice rail (labelled section, cross-rail double-pay guard, frozen amounts, "Paid · pay run / invoice" labels).
- **2026-07-26** — QA job pay (fixed/hourly with precedence), QA "My pay" page, Approval Center decision history + per-item edit/undo/delete with settled-immutability.
- **2026-07-26** — Pay-adjustment correctness: sign-preserving calculator, stamp-based invoice/payroll selection, correct rectification payee (never an admin), pay-change notifications, next-invoice inclusion stamps, settled money immutable.
